#3038. We Shot a Ship Into an Asteroid to See if It Would Move. See the First Images of the Impact

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

It turns out that DART did vastly more damage than anyone had anticipated.

Five years ago, NASA launched its DART spacecraft with the express intent of ramming it into Dimorphos, an asteroid orbiting the larger Didymos asteroid, to see if its trajectory could be changed. The mission was a success, making Dimorphos the first celestial object whose orbit was moved by humans. And now, the European Space Agency has published images showing the damage the mission caused to the asteroid.

The ESA livestreamed the presentation on Tuesday to the delight of cosmic rubberneckers everywhere. The images shown during the livestream were captured by the ESA’s Hera, which was launched in October 2024 and spent two years in deep space with its two CubeSats to measure, map, probe and eventually land on the asteroid.

At the time of the mission, the only instruments that could see the impact and the massive cloud of debris flying off Dimorphos were the Hubble Space Telescope and multiple telescopes on Earth. But they were unable to get a good enough view to see how much damage it did to the asteroid or any other potential changes that occurred.

This means that researchers had to estimate the damage until Hera showed scientists exactly what happened. Spoiler: All of those estimates were incorrect, and by a wide margin.

Hera showed the damage was vastly more significant than originally thought. Scientists believed the impact would generate a 20-meter crater; Hera found that almost the entire end of the asteroid had been blown off, resulting in a crater the size of a football stadium and as wide as Dimorphos itself. This is visible in the above image — as you can see, Dimorphos used to be roughly the shape of an egg. Now, it’s the shape of a deviled egg or a kidney bean, depending on which angle you view it from.

The ESA’s Hera team discovered another neat effect of the impact: Before DART crashed into the asteroid, it was tidally locked to Didymos, much like the moon is to Earth. Scientists expected it to stay that way after the impact, but the images from Hera confirmed that Dimorphos now tumbles randomly, which none of the researchers had on their bingo card.

“Before the DART impact, Dimorphos has always shown its same face to Didymos,” said ESA Hera Mission Specialist Michael Kueppers during the presentation. “And this is what we see now. You can see it looks for a few seconds in the video like regular rotation, but it’s spinning around completely crazy.”

ESA mission manager Ian Carnelli said this surprise was great news because it’ll allow Hera to view Dimorphos on all of its sides, which would have been challenging if it were still tidally locked to Didymos.

The ESA concluded the stream with one final surprise. According to Hera principal investigator Patrick Michel, the Hera team told engineers that it was entirely safe to go to Dimorphos because all the debris would be ejected from the DART impact, with the remaining bits reabsorbed by Didymos and Dimorphos before Hera arrived.

But there is actually quite a lot of debris around the two asteroids. Kueppers says they’ve identified over 100 boulders so far, marked as white dots in the image above. Despite being completely wrong about the debris field, the Hera team was in good spirits since this means there are more things for the team to study.

Kueppers says scientists are “always happy” either way because when they predict something correctly, they get awarded, and when they predict something wrong, they have a challenge to solve. “The problem is for the engineers,” he said.

The three discoveries were made just 20 days into Hera’s mission, which is slated to last six months. The Hera team said more images and discoveries are coming in the next half year.

The point of the DART mission was to see whether humans could deal with a potentially dangerous asteroid by ramming into it or destroying it, as is often seen in Hollywood films like Deep Impact and Armageddon. NASA even frames it as a “planetary defense test.”

The mission was considered an overwhelming success. NASA was hoping to shorten the orbit of Dimorphos by at least 73 seconds, with the most optimal guess being approximately 10 minutes. The orbit was shortened by 33 minutes and 15 seconds. It was such a large discrepancy that scientists studied it and determined that the large amount of debris created extra recoil that amplified the push.

The change in orbit around Didymos also changed the orbit of both asteroids around the sun by about 11.7 microns per second. This doesn’t seem significant, but it adds up to about 1 kilometer every 2.7 years. This is enough to steer potential city-killer asteroids away from Earth’s orbit, given enough time and a big enough battering ram.

Hera’s findings also show the impact was much more devastating than scientists first thought. This provides even more data for NASA and the ESA to work through to plan for such an event if it ever happens.

“As new data come in each day, astronomers will be able to better assess whether, and how, a mission like DART could be used in the future to help protect Earth from a collision with an asteroid if we ever discover one headed our way,” said Lori Glaze, director of NASA’s Planetary Science Division, in a statement from 2022.

#3037. Sandisk’s 8-for-1 Split Is Done. Here’s What Investors Should Do Next.

Sandisk's stock split is finished, so the only question left is whether its AI-fueled flash memory business can hold up as prices cool.

By Micah Zimmerman – Nov 26, 2026 at 4:00PM EST

When a stock splits, the headlines tend to make it sound like something big just happened, but for Sandisk (SNDK -5.57%) shareholders, the real decision starts now that the paperwork is done and the share price looks a lot friendlier.

Sandisk announced its 8-for-1 forward split alongside its fiscal first-quarter results on Oct. 29, giving every holder of record on Nov. 9 seven extra shares for each one they owned. The change became effective on Nov. 19, and split-adjusted trading began the next morning.

A stock split, which is when a company divides each existing share into several cheaper ones, works like cutting a pizza into more slices without adding any cheese. Sandisk itself said that its overall market capitalization and stockholder ownership percentages would not be affected, and it framed the move as a way to make shares more accessible, including to employees buying through its stock purchase plan.

What the split can't do is change the business, so that's where investors should focus.

Sandisk makes NAND flash, which is the memory chip that keeps your data when the power goes off, and AI has turned that once-sleepy product into a hot commodity.

In the quarter that ended Oct. 2, revenue jumped 378% year over year to $11 billion, data center sales rose 61% from the prior quarter, and gross margin, which is the share of each sales dollar left after the cost of making the product, reached 85%.

CEO David Goeckeler said in the release, "We have started fiscal year 2027 with another quarter of strong performance, with robust and growing demand for our products driving outpaced growth in our data center business."

The AI connection is more specific than it sounds, because AI models need fast storage to hold the context of a conversation. On the earnings call, Goeckeler said, "We believe this is a secular dynamic that is expanding NAND's role across the AI stack."

The part I find most reassuring is Sandisk's long-term customer deals, which it calls New Business Models or NBMs. About half of its fiscal 2027 supply and two-thirds of its fiscal 2028 supply are already committed, 95% of those deals are with investment-grade companies, and Goeckeler said the contracts have "become the predominant way we deal with our customers." CFO Luis Visoso went further on the call, saying, "Our NBM pipeline exceeds our supply throughout the end of the decade."

Those contracts matter because flash prices are cooling. Research firm TrendForce now expects NAND contract prices to fall as much as 5% in the first quarter of 2027, with declines spreading across all product categories after that. Concentration adds risk too, since two customers each made up 12% of revenue last quarter.

The stock has already priced in plenty of that worry. Shares closed at $87.71 on Nov. 23, roughly 70% below their split-adjusted 52-week high, and trade at a price-to-earnings ratio of about 5.6. Management guided for split-adjusted earnings of $6.88 to $7.13 per share in the current quarter, which puts the stock near 3 times annualized earnings.

Visoso also cautioned on the call that the March quarter "is normally our weakest sequential quarter," so results may look less dazzling by spring, but the company still bought back $5 billion of stock last quarter, and Visoso said, "Our repurchases reflect our conviction in the long-term value of the business and our disciplined approach to capital allocation."

I think long-term investors who can stomach big swings should consider a modest position in Sandisk, sized for a flash market that could get rougher before it gets better, and add only if the long-term contracts keep holding up as prices fall. The split changed the price tag, but the contracts will decide the value.

Younger workers don't find AI useful, according to a recent survey.

Generation Z might have the lowest morale around AI at work compared to other generations. That’s according to a survey from talent services firm Seramount, which found that Gen Z was the most AI-averse among the generations surveyed about using the technology in their jobs.

Gen Z, which includes those born between 1997 and 2012, expressed the least interest and found the least utility in AI, Seramount found. Only 18% of Gen Z reported an interest in strengthening their AI skills, compared to 36% of Millennials, 46% of Gen Xers, and 43% of Boomers. Gen Z is also using AI less than other generations, by nine percentage points.

Also: OpenAI is offering small businesses free AI support – and up to $8K/mo to experts who help them

“Employers cannot assume their youngest employees will embrace AI on their own,” said Diana Forster, senior director of research at Seramount.

The survey polled responses from over 1,800 US white-collar workers at companies with at least 500 employees.

Around half of Gen Z report weekly AI use (not specific to work), according to a survey from Walton Family Foundation, GSV Ventures and Gallup, but the generation’s skepticism has mounted over the past few years. Even members of Gen Z who use AI daily have less positive views on the technology. In the same study, 42% of Gen Z reported feeling anxious about AI in 2026, a 1% increase from 2025. Last year, 36% of Gen Z respondents reported excitement about AI. In 2026, that percentage dropped to 22%.

Also: These companies are actually upskilling their workers for AI – here’s how they do it

It’s no surprise Gen Z is skeptical about adopting a technology that could be undermining their own career development. The labor market has been particularly harsh to this group of workers, as entry-level job postings have fallen in recent years, and many fear that AI will only exacerbate unemployment. Around 8% of those ages 22 to 24 were unemployed in September, compared to 3% of workers ages 25 to 54, according to data from the Bureau of Labor Statistics.

But with many workplaces still adopting AI, senior employees are trying to prepare Gen Z by getting them on board at work.

“We need to show young workers how AI can strengthen their work, provide clear guidelines and training, and give them opportunities to practice using it and build the skills they’ll need as leaders,” Forster said.

Anyone in the US who bought an iPhone 15 Pro, iPhone 15 Pro Max or iPhone 16 model between June 2024 and March 2025 is eligible to file a claim.

The last day to file a claim in Apple’s $250 million settlement over delayed Siri features is Dec. 21, and payouts could reach $95 per eligible iPhone.

The class covers anyone in the US who bought an iPhone 15 Pro, iPhone 15 Pro Max or any iPhone 16 model between June 10, 2024, and March 29, 2025, the stretch when Apple was promoting a more personal Siri that hadn’t arrived yet. Filing takes a few minutes online, and each qualifying phone gets its own claim.

The iPhone 16 went on sale in September 2024 with Apple Intelligence at the center of its marketing. However, the first AI features didn’t arrive until iOS 18.1, several weeks later, and the most heavily promoted one, a Siri that could pull personal context from your apps, was further delayed in March 2025.

The opt-in form is available only for a limited time, so get your name in the pool before it closes. Eligible buyers have until Dec. 21 to file, and each qualifying iPhone gets its own claim.

Apple settled a false advertising lawsuit brought by Clarkson Law Firm in May, agreeing to pay $250 million to customers who bought some iPhone models during a specified period. The lawsuit alleged that Apple misled customers by promising AI features that didn’t ship with the new devices. Apple denies all of the lawsuit’s allegations and denies that it did anything unlawful or improper.

“Apple has reached a settlement to resolve claims related to the availability of two additional features. “False advertising settlements aim to raise the floor of truth-telling, protect fair competition and increase consumer choice,” said Ryan Clarkson, co-lead counsel on the case and founder and managing partner of Clarkson Law Firm, in a statement to CNET. He claimed it’s the largest false-advertising settlement in history.

Apple lit up its flagship New York City store to resemble Siri’s new color scheme.

The iPhone 15 Pro and iPhone 15 Pro Max are included because they have the processor and memory to run Apple Intelligence features.

It’s estimated that approximately 36 million customers are eligible for this settlement.

When the phones did arrive, Apple Intelligence wasn’t yet ready; its first features didn’t arrive until iOS 18.1, five weeks later.

According to the proposed settlement, “Apple allegedly saturated the market with deceptive ads, inducing consumers to purchase iPhones based on the promise of certain enhanced Siri features.”

Some features of Apple Intelligence did ship soon after the introduction of the iPhone 16 and iOS 18, including Visual Intelligence, Live Translation, Writing Tools, Genmoji and Clean Up. But those weren’t the advanced features Apple highlighted. In fact, the type of intelligent interactions Apple advertised is only starting to show up in Siri AI, which is part of iOS 27, which was released in early September.

According to the settlement, Apple needed to provide the information about affected customers within five days of the settlement approval. As of late July, preliminary approval has been granted by the court.

After the data is provided and verified, a 45-day notice period begins to inform potential consumers that they’re eligible for a payment.

On Aug. 13, US District Court Judge Noël Wise granted approval to push forward the date from Sept. 29, 2027, to Feb. 24, 2027.

Provided approval is granted, the first checks or deposits will arrive sometime within a 60-day window after February next year, depending on court dates and possible extensions.

#3034. You can turn Claude artifacts into Windows and Mac apps now – here’s how

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Anthropic's new 'install as app' feature makes it easy to turn your Claude artifacts into desktop apps on your computer - with some limitations.

Anthropic is making its Claude artifacts available as desktop apps, meaning you can share and more easily access your most-repeated tasks on your computer. The “install as app” feature is available in beta for all free and paid Claude plans and works on both Mac and Windows, but not Linux.

Also: 4 ways Anthropic’s Claude Sheets beat using a Google Sheets connector

Anthropic first released artifacts in 2024. Since then, they’ve become a way to standardize and repeat tasks users do regularly, especially at work. Previously, artifacts ran via your Claude window or in a browser. By splicing them out as distinct desktop apps, you can now run different Claude queries while also using your artifacts locally, or use those apps without opening Claude at all.

“When an artifact is installed as a Mac or Windows app, it gets its own icon and opens in its own window instead of inside Claude. You can share it with others, and it needs your permission before it can interact with your computer,” Anthropic said.

Artifact apps can still access Claude and any connectors you’re using, and they update automatically when you edit the artifact itself in Claude. When apps request something from Claude, though, that goes toward your Claude usage limits, which are lowest for free users.

Anthropic said the app feature is meant to streamline building apps for both developer and non-developer work.

Also: Can AI plan terrorist attacks? US tested ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini, Grok

The update clarifies where AI software for work is headed. Claude is already more of an AI-powered work suite than a single helpful chatbot for many organizations. Evidently, people are using tools like artifacts frequently enough — and simultaneously with other Claude features — that they need those Claude-based tools to function like discrete bits of software on their own.

“If you rely on a tool for work, it should open like the rest of your apps and remind you when something needs your attention,” Anthropic said.

Claude artifact apps don’t have the same functionality as apps you could build using Claude Code, though. Even if you don’t need Claude open for these apps to work, you’ll still need to be signed in to Claude and have internet access, so they’re not an entirely portable or off-grid option.

The feature’s support page also notes that legacy artifacts, “live artifacts from Claude Cowork,” and artifacts generated from templates like Docs, Slides, and Sheets can’t be installed as desktop apps.

First, make sure Claude for Mac or Windows is updated. You can try the “install as app” feature, which is in beta for all free and paid plans, via Claude’s Share menu. Select the Install as app option, which will then download the artifact you chose to your desktop. From there, set permissions as needed.

Permissions range from the default (whatever they are in a browser) to app-specific permissions like sending notifications and full computer access.

Also: I tried replacing Claude Code with a local AI stack that’s completely free

Full computer access means the artifact app can “run programs and read, change, or delete your files without asking each time,” Anthropic said, so proceed carefully. Every time you edit and update the artifact (and thus the app), Claude will ask you to confirm permissions again, so you’ll ideally have regular checkpoints to revise access. Still, there are some guardrails in place: Anthropic only lets you give full computer access to apps you’ve made yourself. For Team and Enterprise plans, that extends to any apps made by your coworkers.

“Anthropic doesn’t review apps before you install them, so only give full access to apps you trust,” the company said.

For Claude Enterprise plans, the install-as-app feature is off entirely until an admin turns it on in Organization settings.

#3032. The Winter Solstice Is Coming: A Guide to the Longest Night

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Next month, we'll experience the shortest daylight hours of the year.

This was taken with the main camera as a ProRaw photo. Notice the lights on the tree, compared to the green hue of the street lighting and blue and pink tones in the sky.

Thanksgiving is almost here, as is Cyber Monday, but soon it’ll be officially winter. The winter solstice is on Dec. 21, when we’ll experience Earth’s shortest, darkest day.

The winter solstice has a few different meanings, so here are all of them. The most common definition, and the one you’re most likely familiar with, is that it denotes the shortest day and longest night of the year on Earth. These times vary depending on your location on the globe, but the further north you live, the less sunlight you’ll get. In the tropics, they’ll get about 12 hours of sunlight. In Sweden, it’ll be closer to 6 hours. In Svalbard, there isn’t any.

The reason this occurs is due to the second definition of the winter solstice: During this time, the North Pole is tilted as far away from the sun as it can get. According to The Farmer’s Almanac, the moment at which the Earth’s axis is physically tilted as much as it can be is at 3:50 p.m. ET on Monday, Dec. 21. After that moment, the process will begin to reverse until the South Pole takes its turn during the summer solstice. At that point, the North Pole will be at its closest tilt to the sun, giving us the longest day and shortest night of the year for the Northern Hemisphere.

Sunset will occur at its earliest time on Dec. 21.

It’ll vary by location. The southern US will have the most sunlight, with Florida getting a little over 10 hours during the winter solstice. The Midwest and Great Lakes portion of the country will get roughly nine hours. The Pacific Northwest can expect a little less than nine hours. Southern Alaska will get about six hours, and the northern areas of Alaska will see around three hours.

If you want to see how your area will fare, Time and Date has a sunrise and sunset tool you can use. Input your zip code into the search bar, and you’ll be able to see your sunrise and sunset times.

The city of Utqiaġvik, Alaska, will have it worse than most in the US: It won’t get any sun at all. The sun won’t rise there again until late January. We hope the people living there packed their vitamin D supplements.

The autumn equinox was just a couple of months ago, so you may be curious about the differences. A solstice occurs when the Earth’s poles are tilted as far away from the sun as they’ll be over the course of one year, which is also one orbit around the sun. The equinoxes take place at the halfway points between each solstice. The autumn equinox takes place in September between the summer and winter solstices, while the spring equinox takes place in March between the winter and summer.

During an equinox, the poles are minimally tilted, and the days are as even as they can be. This also has a couple of other interesting side effects: The sun is pointed directly at the equator during an equinox, and the sun will rise and set directly east and west, respectively.

Humans use the solstices and equinoxes to mark the change of the seasons. There are two equinoxes and two solstices, which mark the start and end dates for Earth’s four seasons.

Decorating with evergreen trees, like with a wreath, is what they used to do to celebrate the winter solstice.

While the solstice is close to the holidays, celebrations for the winter solstice have been around for significantly longer, and some winter solstice traditions have become Christmas traditions as well. One of the most historically famous winter solstice celebrations is Yule, a winter festival originating thousands of years ago among the ancient Norse. It became commonly celebrated in Europe and involved bonfires and massive feasts. Yule celebrations also included decorating with evergreen tree decorations, like holly and ivy.

If these sound familiar, it’s because Christmas co-opted many of those traditions centuries ago, and the two traditions have kind of merged into a single seasonal holiday. The terms Yule and Christmas are often used interchangeably these days in many cultures.

So if you want to celebrate the winter solstice, just do all of the stuff you would normally do for the holidays. Burn a Yule log, decorate a tree, eat a big feast with your family and hang up mistletoe and wreaths — those are all hat tips to traditional winter solstice celebrations.

#3031. What Is a Reconditioning Fee on a Used Car?

Used-car dealers may charge you a reconditioning fee to cover their costs of getting cars ready for sale.

A reconditioning fee is an added dealer charge you might find when buying a used car. It’s intended to cover any costs the dealer incurs to make sure a used car is showroom-ready, from mechanical inspections to detailing.

The reconditioning process varies between dealerships, but typically includes these steps:

A visual exterior inspection. An auto tech does a quick walk-around the vehicle and notes any corrosion, damage or wear.

An interior inspection. The engine and auto interior are examined and tested, and any issues are documented.

A test drive. The car is taken out for a drive to check that it runs well and that the radio, brakes, A/C and other components are all functioning properly.

Service and repair. Any interior or exterior mechanical, functional or cosmetic issues that were found are repaired, including paint, dents, oil changes and major engine work.

Inspection. Once repairs are completed, the car is inspected again to make sure everything looks and functions as it should.

Detailing. This includes deep interior, engine and exterior cleaning as well as any necessary leather refurbishing, bumper repair or wheel restoration.

Reconditioning fees are legal, but that doesn’t mean you have to pay them. Dealers can charge whatever they want for a reconditioning fee, and it’s not uncommon to find them ranging from around $500 to over $2,000.

The good news: Reconditioning fees, like most dealer extras, can be negotiable. Ask the dealer for a breakdown of the reconditioning fee to see what kind of work was done to prepare the car for sale. If the work was minor but the fee is high — for example, if they’re asking for $1,995 but replaced the floor mats only — that’s a good sign it’s a bogus charge.

However, when it comes time for negotiation, don’t just haggle over the reconditioning fee or other individual fees. The best way to negotiate is to focus on the out-the-door price, or the total amount you’re paying for the car. This simplifies the negotiation process and keeps you focused on the true price of your car.

All dealers invest some amount in reconditioning, but those costs are often built into the dealer’s asking price for a vehicle, rather than as a separate charge.

Some dealers opt to add a reconditioning fee to the bill because it allows them to advertise a lower asking price online, which doesn’t include fees. It’s a tactic to get you into their dealership, since the asking price is lower than that of other dealerships.

However, once you arrive, you may find that the out-the-door price is the same — or more — compared with what other dealers are asking.

Some car-buying experts recommend avoiding dealerships that charge reconditioning fees altogether. But remember that the out-the-door price is what matters at the end of the day, so it’s worth comparing prices at multiple dealerships to find the best deal.

Reddit is an online forum where users share their thoughts in “threads” on various topics. The popular site includes plenty of discussion on financial subjects like car buying, so we sifted through Reddit threads to get a sense of how users feel about reconditioning fees. People post anonymously, so we cannot confirm their individual experiences or circumstances.

Reddit users are generally skeptical toward reconditioning fees. Many users view these fees as a bait-and-switch tactic: dealers advertise an attractive price online and then tack on the fee at the dealership, inflating the actual out-the-door cost well above what was listed. Car buyers frequently share stories of being hit with reconditioning fees ranging from a few hundred dollars up to $2,000 or more, often with little or no evidence of what work was actually done.

Car-buying experts on Reddit point out that most dealerships already build reconditioning costs into the sticker price, so if the fee is listed separately, the dealership may be double-dipping. Several users suggest that car buyers ask their dealer for an itemized breakdown of the work done, but most users recommend negotiating the total out-the-door price rather than individual fees.

We tested dozens of Bluetooth headphones in 2026, but two stood out. Here's our top picks.

#3029. Fast Fashion Fiasco: Shein to Pay $100 Million in FTC Complaint

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

The company agrees to the fine after the FTC alleged it deceived US customers.

Shein, one of the biggest fast-fashion brands in the world, has been ordered to pay $100 million by the Federal Trade Commission, which alleges that the Singapore-based online retail giant engaged in deceptive practices that left millions of US customers dissatisfied with its products and policies.

The FTC complaint alleges that Shein failed to follow through on its swift shipping policies, made it difficult for customers to resolve purchase issues and stifled negative customer reviews from seeing the light of day.

The complaint also alleges problems with the company’s supply chain, false information about the origin of the cotton used in its clothes, a failure to communicate shipping updates to customers and a lack of support for those whose packages were late or went missing altogether.

“The company misled millions of consumers about its products, its suppliers and the authenticity of its customer reviews,” Christopher Mufarrige, director of the FTC’s Bureau of Consumer Protection, said in a statement on Tuesday.

Shein has ballooned over the past decade into the ultra-fast-fashion brand we know today, thanks in large part to the impact influencers have had on social media, posting videos trying on clothing supplied by the company.

In 2026, the company is estimated to ship roughly 1 million packages to US customers daily, according to the FTC. That number becomes even more astounding when you consider that Shein, the FTC alleges, employed just five customer service agents to answer calls from American customers.

This isn’t the first time Shein has faced negative scrutiny. Over the years, the company has faced allegations of numerous workers’ rights violations and is the largest polluter in the fast-fashion industry.

According to Yale Climate Connections, the company emitted 16.7 million total metric tons of carbon dioxide into the atmosphere in 2023. Comparatively speaking, that’s more than the pollution output of four coal power plants. Shein has since pledged to improve its treatment of workers and reduce carbon emissions, but scrutiny persists.

The $100 million will be used to compensate customers. And according to the FTC’s proposed order, the company, which earned roughly $50 billion in revenue in 2025, will not be able to continue advertising inaccurate shipping and delivery times; negative reviews will no longer be hidden; and its customer service offering will be bolstered.

“The agreement does not constitute an admission of liability, and we continue to disagree with aspects of the FTC’s characterization of our business and compliance practices,” a Shein spokesperson told CNET.

#3028. Black Friday Porch Pirates Don’t Care About Your Doorbell Camera. Follow This Expert Plan Now

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Soda was stolen moments after delivery. One expert says it's a common tactic.

Doorbell cameras may seem like a good way to make a porch thief think twice, but the reality doesn’t quite match up. Some porch pirates don’t care about being caught on camera as long as they’re able to steal your package, and it’s unlikely that law enforcement will track down the stolen goods.

That’s the case for my fellow CNET editor, Gael Cooper, who had her Amazon delivery stolen a few weeks ago and spotted the thief on camera. It wasn’t expensive tech or a snazzy holiday gift — it was $36 worth of soda.

“When Amazon emailed me that the order was delivered and it wasn’t there, I was very confused,” Cooper said. “When I pulled up the Ring footage, I saw the delivery happen on video, but then, 10 minutes later, a man in a blue hooded coat who’s intentionally hiding his face shows up and grabs the box.”

Package theft cases like this happen year-round, with CNET findings showing that 28% of US adults have had a package stolen, know someone who has or have stopped an attempted theft. And experts predict more stolen packages during the holidays, when thieves expect more deliveries.

So, don’t give thieves a chance. Now’s the time to make a plan to keep your Black Friday and Cyber Monday packages out of sight. But what tactics actually work if doorbell cameras don’t? Here’s what you should know about porch piracy and how to lessen your chances of package theft.

Cooper’s Amazon order was scheduled to arrive between 3 a.m. and 6 a.m. — when it’s dark, but she knew she would be home. But thieves were already out lurking during those hours. Cooper said the thief followed the driver and stole the package. She saw the thief stealing the package on her Ring security camera system.

Greg Zakowicz, Omnisend’s e-commerce and retail advisor, said Cooper’s package-theft incident is fairly typical, unfortunately — especially during the holiday season.

“Thieves know as well as shoppers that orders increase during this time, and often, the packages are more likely to contain more valuable items,” said Zakowicz. “Shoppers during this time look to take advantage of deep discounts for not only gifts but themselves, making the package a potential goldmine for thieves.”

There are a few steps you can take to lessen the likelihood of porch piracy. Zakowicz recommends using options like in-store pickups, post office boxes, in-garage delivery or deliveries to your workplace.

That advice is especially important for expensive tech you plan to have delivered to your home unless someone will be there to grab the package immediately.

“While it may be less convenient, it’s a small price to pay to not have the hassle of gifts and expensive items going missing at the busiest shopping time of the year,” said Zackowicz.

Also ask your retailers not to ship the item in the manufacturer’s packaging. Some retailers, like Amazon and Target, will clearly display this before shipping because it could be an easy hint for thieves to guess what it is — especially if it’s tech. Even though this won’t stop porch pirates from being curious about what’s in the box, it may make them think about the effort and risk versus the potential reward, Zakowicz said.

If you want another option to have packages delivered to your home, consider scheduling your deliveries for a time you know you’ll be available. For instance, you can set up an Amazon Day to have all of your Amazon packages delivered at once so you can plan accordingly.

CNET found that US adults have mixed feelings about how effectively security cameras deter porch pirates: 11% of US adults believe they don’t deter them, while 9% use security cameras to get proof — not prevent it.

Regardless of your home security measures, don’t assume you’re safe from porch pirates just because you have a doorbell camera, and don’t use it as a means of keeping an eye on your package if you’re busy or not home. Instead, when you see motion detection, that’s a sign to grab it right away.

“The most common misunderstanding is that having security cameras will prevent thieves from stealing packages,” said Zakowicz. “Thieves are thieves because being caught is not their biggest worry. They also know porch piracy is not at the top of most officers’ day-to-day public service worries. Often, by the time the report is made, thieves are long gone.”

Unfortunately, thieves may find other ways to steal packages, since their tactics are constantly changing. If that happens to you, there are a few steps you should take right away.

File a police report of the incident first. Be prepared to share information, including a description of the thief, and the make and model of the vehicle, with the police and your community so they can be vigilant of the thief.

But don’t get your hopes up that the thief will be caught. Zakowicz said it’s rare that porch theft cases get solved.

“Police aren’t necessarily dedicating resources to solving porch pirates who stole a new stuffed animal or board game. However, it’s important for them to understand areas seeing an uptick in thefts, as they can be more alert to suspicious activity,” said Zakowicz. And the retailer may request it.

After filing, Zakowicz says to report the theft to the carrier and retailer. Carriers may use the data to assess future likelihood of package theft incidents and it could help instruct delivery drivers to carefully hide packages. Retailers may also be able to assist with a replacement.

Cooper filed a police report with her local police department, which allowed her to upload the Ring video of the theft and acknowledged receipt. She’s not expecting the thief to be caught, but figures that her report could possibly help in some way.

“I live in a pretty safe neighborhood in Amazon’s own hometown, but their delivery trucks are on our street dropping off packages every day,” she said. “It’s got to be a banquet for hungry thieves.”

She reached out to her neighbors to tell them about the theft, and one reported that she, too, had had a package stolen a week earlier.

Cooper says she did not intentionally choose the early-morning drop-off time, but it was the only time Amazon offered for her delivery. Since then, she’s seen Amazon try to offer that same time and has chosen not to order an item rather than be forced to use that dark, quiet delivery slot.

Immediately after the theft, Amazon was less than helpful, routing her to an AI agent who said it understood the situation was upsetting but that it wouldn’t be able to do anything for four days. When she checked back, Amazon agreed to refund her for the item, but kept insisting on labeling the package as “missing” and asked her to report back if she “found” it.

“I hope the driver doesn’t get in trouble for a supposedly ‘missing’ package,” she said. “It unnerves me that she was being followed by a thief. Who knows how long he followed that truck and how many packages he scooped up?”

#3027. The Nasdaq Composite Is in a Correction, but These 3 Dividend Stocks Are Near 52-Week Highs. Here's Why They're Still Worth Buying Right Now.

Johnson & Johnson, Visa, and Coca-Cola have held up during the market's recent turbulence.

By Thomas Niel – Nov 26, 2026 at 4:13PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Johnson & Johnson ›

After slumping throughout November, major indexes like the S&P 500 staged something of a comeback on Monday. Tuesday and Wednesday were a bit more chaotic, but the indexes have held onto most of Monday’s gains so far. Only time will tell whether this week’s action was a dead cat bounce or if it marks the end of the latest stock market pullback. Still, given the recent surge in stocks making new highs, it may be time for investors to consider going defensive.

Across multiple sectors, defensive dividend payers have continued to perform steadily. While each one declined during the sell-off, these three stocks remain near their 52-week highs: Johnson & Johnson (JNJ -0.91%), Coca-Cola (KO -0.03%), and Visa (V +1.83%).

With 64 consecutive years of dividend growth, Johnson & Johnson became one of a small number of Dividend Kings — companies with payout-hiking streaks of 50 years or more — over a decade ago. Yielding around 2% at current prices, this healthcare stock has averaged mid-single-digit annual dividend growth over the past decade.

J&J's latest dividend increase may have only been 3.1%, but the company's ongoing pivot toward a higher-growth business model points to stronger payouts and gains down the road. In particular, the company has pivoted toward building a pipeline of specialty drugs. As Goldman Sachs managing director Asad Haider discussed in an interview with Barron's last month, J&J’s recently released drugs include the immunology treatment Tremfya and the psoriasis treatment Icotyde.

Also, per Haider, promising Johnson & Johnson drug candidates include the bladder cancer treatment Inlexa. And recently approved immunology drug Imaavy could generate up to $5 billion in sales. J&J may trade for around 23 times forward earnings, a big premium to other pharmaceutical stocks, but if its growth pivot persists, the stock could sustain or even grow this valuation.

Coca-Cola is another Dividend King, with a dividend-growth streak also spanning 64 years. At its current share price, Coca-Cola, one of the world’s largest consumer staples companies, has a forward dividend yield of around 2.2%. Its dividend growth has averaged in the mid-single digits in recent years. Still, its strong stock market performance has been driven by its ongoing growth resurgence.

For Q3, the global beverage company reported 7% revenue growth, 12% operating earnings growth, and 8% earnings-per-share (EPS) growth. With this representing improved growth relative to recent years, it's no surprise that Coca-Cola stock has rallied by nearly 40% so far in 2026.

Of course, with this big rally has come a big jump in the stock's valuation. Shares now trade at a forward earnings multiple in the high 20s, on the upper end of the stock's historic valuation range. Yet while this blue chip dividend stock has become pricier as of late, it may have more room to run. Recently, TD Cowen analyst Robert Moskow reiterated his buy rating on Coca-Cola, setting a $112-per-share price target, which is nearly 15% above present price levels. In his research note, the analyst primarily cited the company's deep economic moat and its growth prospects as reasons it could sustain and expand its premium valuation.

Visa may have a forward dividend yield of just 0.75%. Still, with nearly 20 years of consecutive dividend growth, as well as annual payout growth averaging in the mid-double digits during this time frame, I would consider it one of the blue chip dividend stocks.

Visa's latest annual dividend increase came in at 16%, with its quarterly payout rising to $0.78 per share. Two factors suggest that Visa can sustain above-average levels of dividend growth for years to come. First, the stock continues to have a low payout ratio of around 21%.

Second, as the company continues to benefit from the "digitalization of payments" secular growth trend, its top- and bottom-line growth remains steady. For the fiscal quarter and year ending Sept. 30, the company reported net revenue growth of 13% and 15%, respectively, with quarterly and annual non-GAAP (adjusted) earnings per share rising by 17% and 16%, respectively.

#3026. What Are Repositioning Cruises?

We believe everyone should be able to make financial decisions with confidence. While we don't cover every company or financial product on the market, we work hard to share a wide range of offers and objective editorial perspectives.

Other factors include your credit profile, product availability and proprietary website methodologies.

However, these factors do not influence our editors' opinions or ratings, which are based on independent research and analysis. Our partners cannot pay us to guarantee favorable reviews. Here is a list of our partners.

Repositioning cruises move ships between seasonal destinations, often with longer sailings, fewer port stops and lower fares.

A repositioning cruise is a one-way sailing that moves a ship from one region to another. For example, many ships sail in the Mediterranean Sea during the summer and move to the Caribbean Sea for the winter, and that trip between the two is a repositioning cruise.

Repositioning cruises are most common in spring (roughly March to May) and fall (roughly September to November), though they can occur at other times of year.

Because ships are crossing oceans, these trips often include many days at sea. For some travelers, that’s a chance to relax and enjoy the ship’s onboard amenities. Sometimes, these sailings can also be cheaper than traditional cruises.

One key consideration for a repositioning cruise is that the embarkation and disembarkation ports will be different. This means you generally can’t buy a standard round-trip flight to and from the same city. Instead, you’ll need transportation to where the cruise starts and another way home from where it ends, which usually means two one-way flights (or one open-jaw ticket) unless you live near one of the ports. While these one-way sailings may be more affordable than traditional cruises, airfare costs may be higher.

This could be a great opportunity to redeem miles for your flight.

Another feature that sets these cruises apart is that stops along the way tend to include ports that aren’t traditionally part of regular itineraries. These might include islands like Bermuda, the Azores or the Canary Islands, and on some northern routes, Greenland.

Just keep in mind that the main purpose of these sailings is to get to a new region for the next season’s itineraries rather than make daily port stops. So if you’re someone who wants to visit as many places as possible on a cruise, a repositioning sailing may not be the ideal choice.

Repositioning cruises are often longer than standard cruises, commonly running two to three weeks compared with about a week for a typical sailing, which may appeal to travelers looking for an extended vacation. The experience is often more relaxed, with many days at sea and less of a rush to book shore excursions or visit important sites when off the ship.

If there are particular ships with certain amenities — like tattoo parlors or massive waterparks — that you really want to experience, keep in mind that not all ships reposition between regions. Some stay in certain regions year-round.

For ships that do change locations for the season, there are plenty of onboard activities to fill the time. You can enjoy shows, food and wine tastings, organized activities and time at the spa, pool or casino.

Many repositioning crossings are relatively smooth, and modern ships use stabilizers to reduce movement. But since these itineraries cross open ocean rather than staying closer to land, there can be more motion, especially on North Atlantic crossings in fall and winter.

If you tend to get seasick, this could be a dealbreaker. Even if a repositioning sailing is smooth, temperatures can be chilly on the open ocean, especially on northerly or spring and fall transatlantic routes, with strong winds that may make suntanning by the pool less appealing.

You can book one of these sailings in the same way you would book a traditional cruise. But if you’re searching for dates and itineraries on a cruise line’s website, they may be listed as transatlantic, transpacific or Panama Canal sailings rather than repositioning cruises, though some lines do use the term "repositioning." There’s even a website that specializes in rounding up these cruises: RepoCruises.com.

Keep an eye out for themed sailings that focus on topics like wine, theater or music, where fellow guests on the ship may share similar interests. Cruise lines often schedule them on repositioning cruises to spur demand.

Here are some repositioning cruises for 2027. They often take place during shoulder seasons, between the most popular cruising times in places like the Mediterranean or Caribbean.

This journey repositions the ship from Miami, where it sails on Caribbean itineraries, to Seattle, where it will visit ports in Alaska during the spring and summer months. Departing April 8, 2027, the 18-night cruise includes stops at various ports in Colombia, Panama and Mexico, as well as San Diego and Los Angeles, before arriving in Seattle on April 26.

Regent Seven Seas Cruises' all-suite luxury ship Seven Seas Mariner will sail between New York and Lisbon, making stops in Canada, Morocco and the Portuguese islands of Madeira and the Azores.

Departing April 29, 2027, the 16-night journey ends in Lisbon, Portugal, as the ship repositions to Europe, where it will sail various itineraries, including Mediterranean sailings later in the summer season.

Departing Seattle on Sept. 28, 2027, Norwegian Jade will sail to Yokohama, Japan, with stops in Alaska and northern Japan. This is a way to travel across the Pacific with less jet lag, since the journey takes place over several days, with ship clocks typically adjusted gradually (often an hour at a time) during the sailing. Note that the voyage also crosses the International Date Line.

If Greenland has been on your bucket list, this 16-night sailing is a way to get up close and personal with its natural scenery. Departing Reykjavik on Aug. 25, 2027, Azamara Quest will sail from Iceland to the U.S. It will make stops in Greenland, Canada and New England before reaching the Big Apple.

Departing Bergen, Norway, on June 11, 2027, this 28-night sailing aboard Viking Vela sails around Iceland's coast, makes two stops in Greenland and crosses the Atlantic before reaching New York.

#3025. Top Wall Street analysts find these 3 stocks attractive as long-term investments

Investors have been grappling with stock market volatility, weighing elevated oil prices, persistent geopolitical tensions, the Federal Reserve's rate hike and a debate about AI safety. For all that, it helps to look beyond short-term noise and focus on stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors gain clarity, as these experts conduct in-depth research to pick stocks with attractive growth prospects.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Enterprise software and cloud infrastructure company Oracle (ORCL) recently announced better-than-expected results for the first quarter of fiscal 2027. The quarter benefited from solid growth in cloud infrastructure revenue.

After hosting a series of investor meetings with Ken Bond, Oracle head of investor relations following Q1 earnings, Guggenheim analyst John DiFucci reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $400. Oracle remains a Guggenheim Best Idea, he said.

"We continue to believe ORCL is a decade stock, driven by the massive (and profitable) opportunity ahead for AI training and inferencing," said DiFucci. He also cited the traditional Public Cloud business and additional AI-enabled cloud database and cloud applications for his bullish stance. DiFucci expects all the factors to drive revenue and profit acceleration over the next few years.

While Oracle delivered 850 MW of data center capacity in Q1 FY27, Bond cautioned that Infrastructure as a Service revenue in any quarter depends more on the timing of when that capacity comes online, than on the headline MW number. DiFucci expects IaaS growth to stay strong, with full-year growth expected above Q1 FY27's 120% constant currency rate. Management expects total revenue growth to accelerate sequentially throughout fiscal 2027, as the faster-growing infrastructure business makes up a larger share of the mix.

DiFucci addressed concerns regarding customer concentration, saying OpenAI's share of total remaining performance obligations has dropped from 80% last June to about 50% now, and is expected to keep falling as new contracts are signed with new and existing customers.

DiFucci ranks No. 263 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 62% of the time, delivering an average return of about 17.5%. See Oracle Insider Trading Activity on TipRanks.

Space systems and launch services provider Rocket Lab (RKLB) was recently initiated as a buy with an $80 price target by Raymond James analyst Brian Gesuale.

"RKLB has assembled launch, spacecraft, components, payloads and optical communications, with Neutron and Iridium completing medium-lift and applications/spectrum in 2027," said Gesuale.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that with the completion of the Iridium acquisition, Rocket Lab will become the only vertically integrated space company with positive free cash flow (FCF). The deal will add a deployed constellation valued at more than $3 billion, globally coordinated L-band spectrum and about $500 million in earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA) and $300 million in free cash flow (FCF).

Gesuale highlighted RKLB's strong revenue and backlog growth and progress toward positive EBITDA and FCF in 2027/2028. Rocket Lab is moving from a heavy investment phase into monetization, with estimated revenue growth of 59% in 2026, the analyst said, noting that RKLB's backlog has more than doubled to almost $2.4 billion from $1.1 billion in Q3 2025.

Given strong growth projections, the analyst expects RKLB's gross margins to expand from about a 35% trough in 2027 to about 47% by 2030, while R&D spending is estimated to decline from ~35% of sales in 2026 to about 10% in 2028, creating substantial operating leverage as the platform matures.

Gesuale ranks No. 369 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of about 17%. See Rocket Lab Statistics on TipRanks.

J.P. Morgan analyst Doug Anmuth recently upgraded Meta Platforms (META) to buy from hold and raised his price target to $820 from $640. Despite the year-to-date underperformance of META, the 5-star analyst still sees significant upside, as Meta is in the early stages of launching frontier models and AI-powered offerings beyond advertising, particularly the Muse AI agent and Meta Model API access.

"Frontier models are at the core of Meta's product and monetization pipeline over a multi-year period, and its path toward superintelligence," said Anmuth.

In the summer of 2025, Meta set a goal to rebuild its Meta Superintelligence Labs team and deliver frontier AI models within a year, the analyst said. The team appears to have met its goal, rapidly advancing from the rollout of Muse Spark 1.1 in July to Muse Spark 1.3, which is competitive with Claude and GPT models, Anmuth said.

Meanwhile, Meta's Watermelon product will expand opportunities in consumer products, business intelligence, and Family of Apps engagement, the analyst said. The product will enhance internal operations and overall efficiency, said Anmuth, who's confident of Meta's ability to bring consumer-driven AI products to its extensive user base of 4 billion and views its distribution network as a competitive advantage.

While Meta has focused on frontier models and AI product development, Anmuth also sees solid opportunity in its AI infrastructure capacity, which he believes will support future compute needs.

Anmuth ranks No. 832 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of about 9.40%. See Meta Platforms Financials on TipRanks.

#3024. O’Reilly’s Sales to Professional Mechanics Jumped 12.7% Last Quarter. Here’s Why That’s the Clearest Sign of Its Moat.

If you're tired of the AI trade, it's time for a closer look at this auto parts retailer’s booming business.

By Neil Patel – Nov 26, 2026 at 4:30PM EST

10 stocks we like better than O'Reilly Automotive ›

Investors might be jaded by artificial intelligence (AI), which seems to have been an even hotter topic this week with Anthropic's (ANT +1.08%) initial public offering. But it's never a bad idea to consider expanding your horizons and figuring out which other parts of the economy might deserve your capital.

O'Reilly Automotive (ORLY +1.32%) is a sleepy business, but it continues to operate at a high level. Revenue and diluted earnings per share were up 9% and 14%, respectively, year over year in the third quarter. The highlight was how well it's doing with professional customers, with sales to this group rising 12.7% last quarter.

That last data point is a clear signal of O'Reilly's wide economic moat.

Same-store sales (SSS), which strip out new locations, rose 7.2% in Q3, after 5.6% growth a year ago. Management credited "share gains on both sides of our business."

But the professional side is the standout. Sales to repair shops and other pros jumped 12.7% to $2.6 billion, roughly double the 6.2% growth in do-it-yourself (DIY) sales. Professional transactions grew at a mid-single-digit rate. More shops are placing orders, supporting volume, and not just paying higher prices.

Time is money. A mechanic gets paid when a car leaves the lift. Every hour spent waiting on a part is lost revenue. So shops pick the supplier with comprehensive parts availability and fast delivery.

That's where O'Reilly's moat shows up. Its 6,758 stores are backed by 400 hub stores and 32 distribution centers. A regular store carries 24,000 stock-keeping units (SKUs). A hub can hold up to 115,000 SKUs and resupply nearby stores several times a day.

Online sellers can't match that. The company has an intangible asset built over decades. Scale adds a cost advantage through vendor leverage and high-margin private-label brands.

Operating margin widened to 21.4% from 20.7%, including the benefit from a one-time $39 million tariff refund. Management raised its full-year SSS outlook to 5.5% to 6.5% and plans 225 to 235 net new stores this year. Each one makes the network denser.

The biggest long-term threat is electric vehicles (EVs). They have no oil to change and far fewer moving parts. Battery EVs were just 6% of new U.S. vehicle registrations in the third quarter, so the shift is slow. But it's real.

Looking ahead, pro revenue growth is the number to watch. If it continues to outpace DIY, the moat is intact.

O'Reilly has no problem growing profits. But the leadership team is also focused on shrinking the number of shares. The diluted share count fell by 5% over the past year, following $3.5 billion in buybacks through the first nine months of 2026. This ongoing trend is why EPS rises faster than net income.

The industry backdrop helps, too. The average U.S. vehicle was 12.8 years old in 2025, according to S&P Global Mobility, a figure that has steadily increased. Older cars break down more often. That's a powerful catalyst for O'Reilly's 33 straight years (and counting) of SSS growth.

The stock, up 427% in the past decade (as of Nov. 20), is rarely cheap. It trades at a price-to-earnings ratio of 28.6, versus a 23.3 multiple for the S&P 500. For investors with a five-year (or longer) horizon, I believe that premium is fair for a company this consistent.

It also offers quality exposure outside of popular AI stocks. Its fortunes depend on aging cars and busy repair shops, not data center spending. For investors looking to diversify beyond tech, that's a valuable trait. Boring businesses can make exceptional investments.

Atea's BEM/RZR regimen must still prove it can displace Epclusa and Mavyret, and the article leaves that competitive question open.

By The Motley Fool Team – Nov 26, 2026 at 4:54PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Atea Pharmaceuticals ›

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 9, 2026, Atea (AVIR -5.37%) highlighted positive Phase 3 clinical trial data for its hepatitis C virus (HCV) drug candidate. The company is advancing a late-stage oral treatment pipeline while securing external credit to reach commercialization without issuing new equity. Three things management said stand out.

Atea is developing bemnifosbuvir and ruzasvir (BEM/RZR), a fixed-dose oral drug combination for treating HCV. Management believes the candidate offers operational advantages over competing therapies Epclusa and Mavyret, including a shorter eight-week treatment duration for non-cirrhotic patients and lower risk of drug interactions.

Wall Street often views the hepatitis C market as shrinking based on declining sales of older treatments. Management highlighted that newly diagnosed infections continue to outpace treated cases in the U.S., expanding the unaddressed patient base.

Clinical progress across key viral disease programs is accelerating, and leadership is signaling momentum into 2027. Short treatment cycles, broad drug compatibility, and targeted specialty distribution underpin the long-term thesis while management tracks trial readouts and regulatory filings.

Atea plans to report global Phase 3 data in early 2027 before submitting its regulatory filing mid-year, assuming positive results. Execution on clinical milestones and commercial preparations will determine whether the business turns clinical success into market share.

#3021. Tech Stock Sell-Off: Down 13%, Is Microsoft a Buy Now?

While other big tech names are burning through cash on AI, Microsoft is paying for its build-out and still has money left over.

By Patrick Sanders – Nov 26, 2026 at 5:00PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Microsoft ›

Tech stocks have been hit hard this month as investors worry about the enormous amount of money being spent on artificial intelligence (AI), and Microsoft (MSFT -1.25%) has been no exception. Shares are down about 13% from their all-time highs.

But I think Microsoft is being unfairly punished here. While many big tech companies are free cash flow negative due to heavy AI spending, Microsoft has affirmed it will remain in the black at least through fiscal 2027.

That's a big deal. And I think it's one reason why the tech sell-off in November presents an attractive buying opportunity for Microsoft before the end of 2026.

Microsoft is one of the biggest players in the cloud computing space, holding 21% of the global cloud infrastructure market. That makes Azure the second-largest provider of cloud services behind Amazon (AMZN -3.12%).

In Microsoft's fiscal first quarter of 2027, which ended Sept. 30, Azure revenue increased 48% from the previous year. Total Microsoft Cloud revenue was $63.9 billion, up 30% from a year ago, and the company's commercial backlog reached $677 billion.

That's an enormous amount of business coming down the line, so Microsoft is spending freely to ensure it has enough computing capacity to meet demand. It spent $45.2 billion on capex in the quarter. But even with that spending, free cash flow remained positive at $18.6 billion. That's because Microsoft generated $58 billion in operating cash flow during the quarter, up 29% from a year earlier.

That's a big difference from some other tech companies whose AI spending has pushed free cash flow into negative territory.

Despite concerns about AI spending, Microsoft's underlying business is performing very well, thanks to its well-known software business. Nearly 4 million companies use Microsoft 365, including its popular office programs such as Word, Excel, PowerPoint, and Outlook.

Those subscriptions provide recurring revenue, while Microsoft's Copilot AI products provide opportunities to sell more services to existing customers. Microsoft 365 commercial cloud revenue increased 17% during the quarter.

That helped Microsoft's fiscal first-quarter revenue increase 19% to $92.3 billion, while operating income climbed 21% to $45.9 billion. Net income was $36.6 billion, up 32% from a year ago.

I don't expect Microsoft to slow its AI investments anytime soon -- and why should it? Microsoft Azure is generating substantial revenue, and Microsoft can continue to invest in the business while maintaining positive free cash flow.

Now down 13% from its all-time high, Microsoft offers investors an opportunity to buy a highly profitable company with two powerful businesses -- cloud computing and productivity software. I think the market is overlooking Microsoft's ability to finance its AI ambitions, and it's an appealing opportunity for investors before year-end.

#3020. Mortgage Rates Today, Tuesday, November 24: Lower, But …

We believe everyone should be able to make financial decisions with confidence. While we don't cover every company or financial product on the market, we work hard to share a wide range of offers and objective editorial perspectives.

So how do we make money? Our partners compensate us for advertisements that appear on our site. This compensation helps us provide tools and services - like free credit score access and monitoring. With the exception of mortgage, home equity and other home-lending products or services, partner compensation is one of several factors that may affect which products we highlight and where they appear on our site. Other factors include your credit profile, product availability and proprietary website methodologies.

However, these factors do not influence our editors' opinions or ratings, which are based on independent research and analysis. Our partners cannot pay us to guarantee favorable reviews. Here is a list of our partners.

TL;DR: Mortgage rates are down this morning, but could seesaw back up tomorrow.

Another day, another substantial swing. This one's lower, but don't count on it starting a trend: We keep seeing mortgage rates move back and forth, but no one's expecting rates to take a dive. If you're watching this space because you're waiting to refinance, it's unlikely your wait is about to end.

The average interest rate on a 30-year, fixed-rate mortgage dropped to 7.13% APR, according to rates provided to NerdWallet by Zillow. This is 25 basis points lower than yesterday and unchanged from a week ago. (A basis point is one one-hundredth of a percentage point.)

The amount of day-to-day discrepancy we've been seeing prompted me to take a little cruise around the web this morning to see what 30-year rates lenders are advertising. Mortgage lenders' rates are never identical, but when we're seeing rapid change, the gaps between lenders tend to widen — and that's for sure what I saw. I looked at six different lenders, and saw a nearly 70-basis-point difference between the highest and lowest APRs. (And yes, that was looking for the same loan amount, location and so on.)

My point is, whether you're looking to buy or refinance, make sure to shop multiple lenders. You're the same candidate bringing the same financials to each one, but you could end up with extremely different rate quotes. And it pays to compare. Freddie Mac estimates that comparing at least two lenders could save you up to $600 per year in interest. Compare four or more, and that savings is over $1,200 annually.

Here are the average APRs we're seeing today, as well as movement over the past month for context. For more, keep reading below the charts.

» Take the next step: Compare mortgage rates from NerdWallet’s top lenders

Mortgage rates are constantly changing, since a major part of how rates are set depends on reactions to new inflation reports, job numbers, Fed meetings, global news ... you name it. For example, even tiny changes in the bond market can shift mortgage pricing.

Mortgage rates may be lower today, but like I mentioned above, it's been more of a seesaw than a ski slope — and we may be about to hit the high side again. The latest Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE) price index is due out tomorrow morning. Recently, when rates have moved higher, inflation's been in the mix.

Strong inflation's generally bad for borrowing, including to buy a home, because higher interest rates are the remedy. Raising the federal funds rate (the short-term borrowing rate the central bankers of the Federal Reserve actually set) is the Fed's main tactic for managing inflation. While the Fed doesn't set mortgage rates, its decisions influence the entire economy. That includes mortgage lenders, who tend to adjust rates ahead of its decisions.

We already know how PCE's likely to go, since it's the last of the three big inflation measures to be released. We've already gotten last month's Consumer Price Index and Producer Price Index. October's CPI came in worse than predicted, showing widespread price increases. (When most of the increase is driven by one area, it's easier to be optimistic that inflation can be contained.) And October’s PPI showed wholesale inflation climbed at its fastest pace in almost four years. PPI measures the price businesses pay for things, so it's often a preview of what consumers could face once those costs get passed along.

Predictions for October PCE aren't great, so at this point the hope is that the numbers aren't even worse than expected. And again, PCE's out tomorrow morning. We'll know whether we're getting seconds (actually, thirds) of bad October inflation news well before the turkey hits the table on Thursday.

Markets are currently anticipating a quarter-point hike at the Fed's December meeting. Even though that'll remain the most likely outcome, a poor showing from PCE could spark speculation about a half-point hike. That probably won't be good for mortgage rates.

But back to the upcoming holiday: Markets will be closed on Thanksgiving, and Black Friday's a half day, so we'll all have a bit of a break this week. Mortgage rates don't move much when the markets aren't moving. I won't be here to talk to you about mortgage rates, either, 'cause NerdWallet gives us Thursday and Friday off. Happy Thanksgiving!

» Learn more: How the Fed affects mortgage rates

Refinancing might make sense if today’s rates are at least 0.5 to 0.75 of a percentage point lower than your current rate (and if you plan to stay in your home long enough to break even on closing costs).

With rates where they are right now, you could start considering a refi if your current rate is around 7.63% or higher — but there just aren't too many people for whom that's true.

Also consider your goals: Are you trying to lower your monthly payment, shorten your loan term or turn home equity into cash? For example, you might be more comfortable with paying a higher rate for a cash-out refinance than you would for a rate-and-term refinance, so long as the overall costs are lower than if you kept your original mortgage and added a HELOC or home equity loan.

If you're looking for a lower rate, use NerdWallet's refinance calculator to estimate savings and understand how long it would take to break even on the costs of refinancing.

» Time to refi? Compare refinance rates from NerdWallet’s top lenders

There is no universal “right” time to start shopping — what matters is whether you can comfortably afford a mortgage now at today’s rates.

If the answer is yes, don’t get too hung up on whether you could be missing out on lower rates later; you can refinance down the road. Focus on getting preapproved, comparing lender offers, and understanding what monthly payment works for your budget.

NerdWallet’s affordability calculator can help you estimate your potential monthly payment. If a new home isn’t in the cards right now, there are still things you can do to strengthen your buyer profile. Take this time to pay down existing debts and build your down payment savings. Not only will this free up more cash flow for a future mortgage payment, it can also get you a better interest rate when you’re ready to buy.

» Is now a good time to buy? See NerdWallet’s analysis

If you already have a quote you’re happy with, you should consider locking your mortgage rate, especially if your lender offers a float-down option. A float-down lets you take advantage of a better rate if the market drops during your lock period.

Rate locks protect you from increases while your loan is processed, and with the market forever bouncing around, that peace of mind can be worth it.

🤓 Nerdy Reminder: Rates can change daily, and even hourly. If you’re happy with the deal you have, it’s okay to commit.

» Stay informed: Check out NerdWallet's mortgage news hub for all our latest coverage.

The rate you see advertised is a sample rate — usually for a borrower with perfect credit, making a big down payment, and paying for mortgage points. That won't match every buyer's circumstances.

Even two people with similar credit scores might get different rates, depending on their overall financial profiles.

#3019. Janus International Group: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

Steel costs and rate pressure are delaying the storage builds that feed Janus's volumes, and that tension is the part long-term holders must weigh.

By The Motley Fool Team – Nov 26, 2026 at 5:07PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Janus International Group ›

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings conference call, held on Nov. 10, 2026, Janus International Group (JBI +1.34%) lowered its full-year revenue guidance to between $855 million and $865 million. A sharp slowdown in self-storage development tests whether the manufacturer can defend its margins through cost cuts and technology sales when construction activity turns sluggish. Three takeaways from management's comments show how the business is adapting.

Higher input costs and financing rates squeeze developers' margins, forcing customers to delay construction of new facilities. That pause directly cuts into delivery volumes for self-storage building components.

When top-line volume drops, structural cost reductions help protect profitability. Management cut expenses across operations and manufacturing to align its cost footprint with lower market demand.

Management is expanding its Noke Smart Entry platform, a technology-enabled access control system for self-storage facilities, to build recurring digital revenue. Adding tech content to existing sites creates recurring revenue opportunities beyond physical steel doors.

Demand remains under pressure across North American self-storage channels, and market conditions show no immediate signs of recovery. Janus relies on its broad customer distribution and technology adoption to defend market share while maintaining pricing discipline.

Disciplined capital allocation and cash generation remain central priorities while new project starts lag.

#3018. Should You Buy Tesla Stock Before December?

Merger talks might jolt Tesla stock by year-end, or they might now.

By Prosper Junior Bakiny – Nov 26, 2026 at 5:15PM EST

Tesla (TSLA -1.18%) has had a challenging year. Despite its core electric vehicle (EV) business often performing better than expected, partly due to soaring oil prices, the company's shares have significantly lagged broader equities. However, the year isn't over, and there is a chance Tesla could finish 2026 with a bang, given ongoing developments. With that said, should investors consider buying the stock before December?

Tesla's CEO, Elon Musk, now leads two publicly traded corporations, the other being Space Exploration Technologies (SPCX +1.30%), which went public in June. Rumors of a potential merger between Tesla and SpaceX have been circulating since before SpaceX was public, but a comment Musk made during SpaceX's third-quarter earnings call added fuel to the fire.

Musk said that SpaceX and Tesla have formed independent special committees to discuss a potential merger between the two corporations. And more recently, five board directors from both companies have reportedly been discussing a potential all-stock merger that would make the combined entity worth about $3.5 trillion, with a deal possible by year-end. Importantly, this hasn't been confirmed by either company. But it's still worth figuring out the potential benefits and risks of this proposed acquisition.

Suppose SpaceX acquires Tesla at a 20% premium in an all-stock deal: SpaceX would have to issue a substantial number of new shares, thereby significantly diluting existing shareholders. Tesla's stock might rise, at least initially, while SpaceX's could decline unless investors believe the merger will generate enough synergies to justify the acquisition premium.

There are indeed some reasons to think that the combined entity could create more value than either would on its own. For instance, both Tesla and SpaceX are investing massive sums in artificial intelligence (AI). As a single entity, they could eliminate redundant investments and lower costs, while leveraging their larger size to negotiate better deals with suppliers.

Also, some of Tesla's work, including its energy infrastructure and the Optimus humanoid robot, could be of immense value to SpaceX. So, that could be good news for the business' long-term prospects, although whether the proposed combined entity will, on balance, create more value is by no means guaranteed. However, one major source of uncertainty will likely come from regulators. The deal will probably attract significant attention from lawmakers, at least some of whom may try to stop it.

Whether they succeed or not, it's hard to predict the impact on Tesla and SpaceX. And even as a combined entity, the company will be significantly exposed to regulatory risk across businesses such as space launch, satellite-based connectivity, EVs, and robotaxis.

Putting aside the acquisition talks, Tesla's own business has somewhat attractive prospects. The company's work in the robotaxi industry looks promising. Tesla launched the Cybercab, a self-driving vehicle with no steering wheel, no pedals, and no mirrors, earlier this year. Provided it can scale its Cybercab fleet and capture a decent share of the market over the next five to 10 years, the robotaxi business could meaningfully contribute to the company's revenue and profits.

And Tesla does have an advantage over other robotaxi leaders. The company has a growing number of subscriptions to its Full Self-Driving software, which it offers to EV owners for $99 per month. A growing FSD ecosystem grants the company access to more data to train and improve its software. And as Tesla has pointed out, the FSD software is becoming one of the reasons people buy Teslas. That's great news for the company's future.

But one of Tesla's problems is valuation. At 157.3x forward earnings, the market has already priced in significant success from the company's robotaxi ambition. So, I'd be skeptical about buying Tesla's shares at current levels. I'd prefer a much lower price. The potential merger with SpaceX changes very little, in my view, and actually adds significantly more uncertainty to the mix.

The combined entity (if a merger does happen) will certainly be worth serious consideration, but investors with an average tolerance for risk and volatility shouldn't buy Tesla stock right now, whether or not they expect the company to merge with SpaceX.
