#2963. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Uncertainty in the Middle East, elevated Treasury yields and fears about an AI bubble continue to keep global markets on edge. Given this volatile backdrop, investors seeking stable income can consider enhancing their portfolios by adding dividend-paying stocks.

However, the vast universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Tracking the ratings of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as their recommendations are backed by in-depth research.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Chord Energy is an independent exploration and production company with long-lived assets primarily in the Williston Basin. With a quarterly base dividend of $1.30 per share, CHRD offers a dividend yield of 3.67%.

In a research note on third-quarter expectations, RBC Capital analyst Scott Hanold reiterated a buy rating on CHRD stock with a price target of $175. "We model production near the top of guidance and continue to see strong performance from new wells and base production," said Hanold.

Specifically, the five-star analyst expects Q3 2026 production at 283 thousand barrels of oil equivalent per day, or Mboe/d, in line with the Street's consensus and near the high end of the 278 to 284 Mboe/d guidance. Hanold said his Q3 2026 earnings per share estimate increased to $5.29 due to lower non-cash depreciation, depletion and amortization.

Meanwhile, cash flow per share estimate moved lower to $13.59 to reflect Hanold's update based on the current backdrop and commodity mark-to-market. He expects Chord Energy to report capital spending of $375 million for Q3 2026, slightly above the Street's estimate of $369 million.

Overall, Hanold remains bullish on CHRD, given its best-in-class balance sheet. He expects a free cash flow payout of 80% in the third quarter, with the possibility of an upside. Hanold expects investors to focus on management commentary on 2027 capital plans, Williston M&A opportunities, and long-term performance of 4-mile wells.

Hanold ranks No. 310 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 14.9%. See Chord Energy KPIs on TipRanks.

Williams, an energy infrastructure company that delivers natural gas, is the next dividend pick. The company recently completed the $5.5 billion acquisition of Momentum Midstream, which will expand its natural gas infrastructure in the Haynesville to meet growing Gulf Coast LNG, power and industrial demand. At a quarterly dividend of $0.5250 per share, Williams offers a yield of 2.89%.

In her latest research note, RBC Capital analyst Elvira Scotto reiterated a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $87. Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, the analyst said that she expects WMB to announce strong quarterly performance.

Scotto raised her estimates to reflect insights from the quarterly catch-up call, the completion of the Momentum Midstream acquisition, and mark-to-market quarter-to-date commodity prices. She expects WMB to report Q3 2026 adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation and amortization of $2,036 million, which is modestly above the Street's consensus estimate.

Furthermore, Scotto expects Williams' Transmission, Power & Gulf segment to increase quarter-over-quarter, driven by the startup of the Socrates power project and favorable natural gas liquids, or NGL, margins.

Scotto noted that WMB stock has underperformed due to backlash related to data centers and investor anticipation of another Power Innovation project. She expects the company to announce at least one more project before the end of this year, potentially with a new customer and a contract duration of 15 to 20 years, which would be a positive catalyst for the stock.

Scotto ranks No. 175 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been profitable 67% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.2%. See Williams Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

EOG Resources, a crude oil and natural gas exploration and production company, will hold its third-quarter earnings call on Nov. 6. At a quarterly dividend of $1.02 per share, EOG offers a dividend yield of 2.75%.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Jefferies analyst Lloyd Byrne reiterated a buy rating on EOG Resources stock and increased his price target to $185 from $175. The analyst expects the company's Q3 cash flow per share to exceed the Street's consensus by 13%, driven by better-than-expected production.

Byrne expects crude oil production, excluding the UAE, to reach 551 Mboe/d, which is at the high end of guidance (546-551 Mboe/d) and above the Street's projection of 548 Mboe/d, driven by continued growth in the Utica.

Furthermore, Byrne expects cash operating expenses to come in at $10.27 per barrel of oil equivalent, below the midpoint of guidance of $10.05 to $11.35, helped by lower lease operating expenses. Overall, the analyst expects EOG to deliver free cash flow of $2.65 billion, supporting another quarter of buybacks near the minimum 70% shareholder return threshold and implying about $1.5 billion in share repurchases.

"As a first indication of the '27 plan, we expect EOG will message another year of mid-single-digit (~3.5%) growth on a flat capital budget (~$6.55bn), which is above the Street modeling 1% oil growth at $6.6bn," said Byrne.

Byrne ranks No. 303 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 18.5%. See EOG Resources Statistics on TipRanks.

#2956. Thinking About Investing in Anthropic? Make This 1 Portfolio Move First

There's nothing wrong with buying Anthropic, as long as you understand and are prepared for the risks.

By Reuben Gregg Brewer – Nov 26, 2026 at 8:15PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Anthropic ›

Anthropic (ANT +1.08%) raised a record $93.5 billion when it held its initial public offering (IPO) in late November 2026. That instantly made it one of the world's largest companies, one of the largest technology companies, and one of the biggest direct plays on artificial intelligence (AI). If you have set your sights on buying the stock, you aren't alone. But before you jump aboard, make sure you make this one portfolio move (and this one mindset move that goes with it).

At this point, it seems like everyone agrees that artificial intelligence will change the world. So it is completely understandable that you would want to buy one of the biggest companies in the AI space after it goes public. However, you need to step back and think about the risks. And not the risks that AI may pose to humanity. The risks inherent in new technologies.

Going back to the last big technology, the internet changed the world, but not every internet company turned out to be a big winner. For example, Yahoo! was once an industry giant, but it is now just a shadow of its former self. But the fact that not all companies involved in a technological revolution survive is nothing new. There are only a handful of major automobile manufacturers around today because many more went bankrupt or got swallowed up by larger competitors. Trains went through the same cycle.

Look at any technological advance, and you'll see something similar. Lots of companies rush in, but only a small number survive and thrive. Anthropic is a leader today, but it may not be one of the survivors over the long term. Which is why the one thing you need to do before buying it is make sure your portfolio is diversified. If all you own is technology stocks, even if you own 100 of them, you are not diversified.

To be diversified, you should own stocks of as many as 50 companies across various sectors. No single investment should make up a disproportionate share of your portfolio. While there's no exact number here, 50 stocks suggests that each stock should be around 2% of your portfolio. From a sector allocation perspective, tech makes up around 37% of the S&P 500 index (^GSPC -0.25%), which should probably be the high end of your range.

The key is that you are trying to limit the risk that Anthropic flames out in a blaze of glory. Notably, the company's IPO prospectus highlights that it is losing money and will likely continue to do so. It already has substantial indebtedness. And it may need to raise even more capital in the future. That's a short list of the risks that don't even consider the actual technology the company produces. In fact, the stock has already been highly volatile, dipping below its IPO price.

Owning Anthropic is fine, but the best place for it is in a well-diversified portfolio, where the investment's inherent risks can be offset by other holdings. This brings up another important consideration: thinking long term. Anthropic is at the forefront of AI, a new technology that is still evolving. Nobody knows what that technology will look like in a year, let alone a decade. However, that's exactly why you should go in thinking that Anthropic will be a long-term holding. Trying to time its ups and downs will be just as difficult as timing any other investment, and the failure rate is likely to be high.

It is exciting to own new companies in new sectors, but it comes with risks. You have to understand those risks and make accommodations for them. Which is why the number one portfolio move here is diversification, backed by a long-term investment mentality. It is perfectly fine to buy Anthropic, but only if you go in fully understanding and preparing for the risks that it entails.

#2949. Stocks Are Plunging Right Now. Here's What History Says Investors Should Do.

Stocks briefly swung into correction territory in November; here's why staying invested may be a smart choice.

By Rick Orford – Nov 26, 2026 at 9:00PM EST

10 stocks we like better than S&P 500 Index ›

Watching stocks plunge can make even the most seasoned investors nervous. Indeed, the S&P 500 (^GSPC -0.25%) and Dow Jones Industrial Average (^DJI +0.33%) closed in correction territory on Nov. 20. From there, the Nasdaq Composite (^IXIC -0.62%) surged 6.5%, and the S&P 500 jumped 4.7% on Nov. 23.

But now, some markets are turning lower again. So, before rushing to sell, it's worth looking at what history tells us about market downturns and why staying invested may be the smarter move.

Perhaps the most tempting reaction during a market sell-off is to sell everything and "wait for the market to improve." The problem with the strategy is that it requires investors to get two decisions right: when to sell and when to buy back in.

In fact, seven of the market's 10 best trading days over 20 years occurred within 15 days of its 10 worst days. As a result, investors who sell during periods of extreme volatility risk realizing temporary losses, while missing out on the recovery.

The latest bout of volatility is a good example.

Tech stocks were hit hard in November as investors questioned whether the enormous investments in artificial intelligence would ever generate strong returns. Indeed, concerns about semiconductor demand, regulatory issues, and a possible slowdown in AI spending added to the pressure.

These developments are certainly worth watching, but the broader U.S. market has repeatedly shown its ability to recover and reach new highs over time.

Of course, staying invested doesn't mean blindly holding just any stock regardless of what's happening in the markets. Broad sell-offs can create buying opportunities, but investors need to distinguish between quality companies whose stocks are "cheap" and businesses that are fundamentally weakening.

For example, the recent concerns surrounding AI are worth considering. Investors have become more sensitive to the enormous capital expenditures (capex) required to build data centers, develop advanced chips, and expand compute infrastructure. Plus, questions about monetization, financing, and competition have contributed to volatility across the tech sector.

To be fair, these are real concerns. Not every AI company will become a long-term winner, and some of today's valuations have become difficult to justify. However, that doesn't necessarily invalidate the broader AI investment opportunity.

Specifically, companies with durable competitive advantages, strong balance sheets, recurring revenue, and credible paths to profitability may still be well positioned to benefit from AI adoption.

Beyond individual stocks, investors can also look at how balanced their overall portfolios are.

A steep correction can expose just how much risk an investor has in a particular industry, company, or asset class. That's why diversification and rebalancing matter a lot.

To start, investors can review whether their current portfolios still align with their financial goals, time horizon, and risk tolerance. For example, someone who has become overly concentrated in high-growth tech stocks may want to diversify into other, less-correlated sectors. On top of that, holding a broader mix of businesses across industries, alongside assets like bonds and cash, can help reduce dependence on any single market theme.

Rebalancing can also have an opposite effect. In practice, when stocks fall, their weighting in a portfolio may decline. Investors with enough liquidity could consider gradually adding to certain asset classes and bringing their allocations closer to their original targets.

Ultimately, staying disciplined and avoiding emotional decisions can help investors weather volatility and stay focused on the long term.

History has shown that panic selling can be costly, especially as investors risk missing out on the eventual recovery. Instead of trying to time the market, investors can focus on what they can control. This could mean maintaining a diversified portfolio, investing consistently, and revisiting their holdings.

After all, during corrections, patience and discipline may actually be more valuable than trying to predict what happens next. In the end, staying invested may simply be the best move for long-term investors.

#2939. Nvidia Is Down 35% From Its All-Time High, Tied for Its Biggest Drop Since the AI Boom Began. Is It a Buy?

By Keithen Drury – Nov 26, 2026 at 10:00PM EST

The Nvidia (NVDA -3.91%) fallout has truly been incredible. At its peak, Nvidia's market cap was nearly $5.8 trillion. Now, it's about $3.6 trillion. That's more than $2 trillion in losses, and only five companies in the world have a market cap greater than that. That's hard to wrap your mind around, but Nvidia's loss of value since CEO Jensen Huang resigned and returned is unparalleled.

Nvidia is now down around 35% from its all-time high. That's significant because it is now tied for the deepest sell-off Nvidia has faced since the AI build-out kicked off in 2023. The previous deepest sell-off occurred in April 2025 when tariff fears drove the stock market down. However, that peaked at about a 35% sell-off.

With Nvidia at its cheapest point in a long time, is it worth buying? Or do investors have more to worry about? Let's take a look.

Even before Huang resigned as CEO, there was trouble with Nvidia's stock. The market was a bit concerned about industry-wide AI spending and assumed it could not last forever, so it discounted Nvidia's stock. The idea here was that AI spending would decrease, harming Nvidia. However, that is looking like a bad assumption.

During the company's Q3 earnings call, interim CEO and current CFO Colette Kress increased revenue growth guidance for next year from 70% to 75%. That is due to extreme demand for AI from hyperscalers and won't change regardless of who leads Nvidia.

That is great news for shareholders, and if that growth comes to fruition, there's no way that Nvidia will stay at its current level.

The reality is that Huang built Nvidia into such a giant and dominant company that it can run itself for a few years. While there were concerns about what the future of Nvidia looks like without him, those fears are now gone with him back in the CEO role. Still, Huang must testify before Congress to address some of the events that happened as CEO and explain what controls they have on chips or trade secrets that shouldn't have fallen into China's hands, but might have. Huang and Nvidia have a ways to go before they're in the clear, but Nvidia's expected growth isn’t changing.

But where will next year's growth take it?

For this fiscal year, Wall Street analysts estimate its revenue will total $424 billion. If Nvidia hits that figure (the company has a strong track record of exceeding estimates) and grows by 75% next year, that would put its revenue at $742 billion. One of Nvidia's impressive characteristics is its profit margin, which has sat at 62% over the past 12 months. If the company can maintain that level, it would generate $460 billion in profits. That's right, Nvidia's profits should be larger next year than its revenue is this year.

That's a simply incredible projection, and given how aggressive the AI hyperscalers are about scaling up computing power, I think it's realistic. With that money, Nvidia could do all sorts of things, including repurchasing a large amount of outstanding shares. This may be a viable option because Nvidia's stock is cheap right now, trading for a mere 9 times next year's earnings estimates.

NVDA PE Ratio (Forward 1y) data by YCharts

Few stocks in the market trade at that level and are growing at a market-beating pace, let alone one that is expected to grow at a 75% pace next year.

Nvidia is one of the greatest values on the stock market right now; I think it's a no-brainer buy, and investors should be scooping up shares while they are massively discounted.

#2930. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

#2928. Amazon Lost 40 Satellites on a Boeing-Lockheed Rocket in November. SpaceX Is the Only Company That Can Fix Amazon’s Problem. Is the Stock a Buy Near $130?

The failed launch is a blow to Amazon's plans, but SpaceX isn't going out of its way to help other companies.

By Reuben Gregg Brewer – Nov 26, 2026 at 10:33PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Space Exploration Technologies ›

Amazon.com (AMZN -3.12%) was hoping that the United Launch Alliance's (ULA's) Nov. 15 space trip would end with it having another 40 satellites in orbit. About 10 minutes into the launch, that dream went up in smoke, as the spacecraft exploded. That's a problem for Amazon, which has another 38 launches booked with ULA.

Space Exploration Technologies (SPCX +1.30%) could lend a hand, but it probably won't. Which is why more aggressive investors might find SpaceX of interest. Here's what you need to know.

SpaceX is at the front of the pack in the space race. And it really is a race, as companies attempt to stake out their position in the still-emerging space industry. Amazon has basically made a huge bet with SpaceX competitor ULA, which is currently looking less desirable. The cost of losing the 40 satellites is bad enough, but it isn't the end of the issue. Amazon will need to build 40 more to replace them, and then get those satellites into space. But ULA's setback is likely to lead to delays on that front, as it works out what happened. This could complicate ULA's plans to increase its launch cadence.

This is good news for SpaceX because Amazon is attempting to create a satellite system to compete with SpaceX's Starlink. But there's more to the story here. SpaceX has been highly successful in its launches, sending many spacecraft into orbit. It could offer its services to other companies, including Amazon. To be fair, it does. But it looks like SpaceX is increasingly using its launches to orbit its own satellites.

Basically, SpaceX holds a leading position in both its Starlink service and its launch services. Other companies are playing catch-up, and that's not working out particularly well. Thus, SpaceX's lead is maintained, if not extended, in both niches of the industry with each successful launch. This is an industrial giant that is flexing its muscles.

But it is hard to value SpaceX as a business. For starters, it has only been public for a short while, so there's not much history to go on. But even if you take the strong third-quarter 2026 revenue growth of 161% and the company's positive earnings of $0.35 per share into account, you can't ignore the massive capital spending that's going on. Or the massive capital spending that is likely to be needed in the future.

To put a number on that spending, SpaceX spent $19.6 billion on capital investments in the third quarter alone. Roughly $1.6 billion of that went to its space operations, $1.5 billion to connectivity, and a shockingly large $16.6 billion to the company's artificial intelligence division. None of that spending was optional, and there's likely to be plenty more capital investment to follow in the years ahead.

And while SpaceX reported positive earnings in the third quarter, its year-to-date total through the first three quarters of 2026 remains negative. In other words, SpaceX is a money-losing business spending huge sums to grow. That's a high-risk proposition that only the most aggressive investors should be considering.

If you are going to buy SpaceX, you have to believe that the company will remain an industry leader. This is the kind of company you buy and hold for the long term. So far, at least in the space and satellite connectivity sectors, it remains top of class, with little indication of that changing anytime soon given the failed ULA launch.

Notably, Wall Street is generally positive about the future, assigning SpaceX an average target price of $256 per share, well above the $127 per share as of this writing. However, volatility is likely to be extreme, as it has already been even over the stock's short trading history. If you can't handle that, you shouldn't own the stock.

Management is betting that capacity investments and margin pressure today will unlock pricing power and profitability gains tomorrow.

By The Motley Fool Team – Nov 26, 2026 at 11:13PM EST

Rapid growth also creates near-term friction, as management trades short-term margins for long-term production capacity. Three takeaways from the call reveal how management handles this rapid expansion.

Rapidly scaling production creates bottleneck risks that hurt efficiency. Management didn't hesitate to accept higher labor and outsourcing costs to keep pace with customer schedules rather than miss critical delivery dates.

Long-term expansion relies on durable enterprise demand. The heating, ventilation, and air conditioning (HVAC) segment saw organic backlog expand sharply as tech customers expanded AI-related infrastructure projects.

SPX Technologies expanded its addressable market by purchasing FIS Water, a specialized business providing water management solutions. Integrating these products with existing cooling towers deepens customer relationships across high-growth end markets.

Demand across power, industrial, and technology sectors remains robust, and management isn't hiding its enthusiasm for ongoing market expansion. The company holds strong proprietary intellectual property, flexible secondary sourcing, and established global distribution channels. Management focuses on production throughput and pricing discipline to offset temporary input cost inflation.

Capacity additions at key facilities will help internalize component fabrication as new assembly lines reach full operating efficiency. That transition positions the enterprise to improve margins while fulfilling multiyear commitments across power and cooling markets.

#2923. History Says Investing $200 a Month in This Vanguard ETF Could Build Life-Changing Wealth Without a Single Stock Pick

Worried about trying to pick stocks during a stock market crash? No problem. VOO has you covered.

10 stocks we like better than Vanguard S&P 500 ETF ›

The recent sell-off in artificial intelligence (AI) stocks triggered a correction in the stock market. The S&P 500 index (^GSPC -0.25%) fell by more than 10% from its recent highs and is struggling to remain in positive territory for the year.

During times like these, it's easy for investors to worry that they're picking the wrong stocks. Or worry that the stock market is a bad place to put their money -- what if the S&P 500 goes down by another 10% next month?

If you want to invest in the stock market, times like these are actually some of the best moments to put money to work, because many stocks are trading at lower prices than just a few weeks ago.

And you don't have to pick stocks to make great money in the stock market. Just buying a low-cost index fund like the Vanguard S&P 500 ETF (VOO -0.28%) can deliver life-changing returns in the long run.

Let's look at just how much you could make from steady $200 monthly investments in VOO.

The Vanguard S&P 500 ETF (VOO) is one of the most popular ways to invest in the stock market because it charges ultra-low fees (0.03% expense ratio) and it holds 500 of the largest, most important publicly traded stocks in America. When you buy VOO, you're getting the entire S&P 500 index: 500 of the most profitable large-cap stocks. When America's largest publicly traded companies do well, so will you.

This index changes day by day as some stocks go up and others go down, weighted by market cap. For example, if one company has a bad month, the S&P 500 index will hold a slightly lower amount of its stock than it did a month ago. If another company performs better, its stock will account for a larger percentage of the S&P 500 by weight.

This means that the S&P 500 index is constantly sorting the "winners" and "losers" for you. This doesn't mean that the S&P 500 index always goes up or is guaranteed to deliver positive returns. But because S&P 500 ETFs like VOO hold 500 stocks, they are broadly diversified. That tends to make their performance less volatile over the long run than that of individual stocks or sector-specific ETFs.

In the past five years, the Vanguard S&P 500 ETF has delivered annualized returns of about 12%.

But if you're worried that the recent stock market correction will mean lower returns in the future, keep this in mind: over the long run, since 1928, the S&P 500 has delivered annualized returns of 9.98%. That's lower than the past five years, but still a strong return.

Let's assume that VOO can continue delivering 9.98% annualized returns over the coming years -- even if there are bear markets along the way. And let's assume that someone starts investing $200 a month at age 30 and keeps investing for 37 years until their Social Security retirement age of 67.

If you invested $200 per month in VOO and earned 9.98% average annual return of 9.98%, after 10 years, your investments would grow to $38,213. After 20 years, you'd have $137,148. After 30 years, you'd have $393,294. And after 37 years, your investments would be worth $788,199.

#2918. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

#2917. Prediction: This Will Be Anthropic’s Stock Price by the End of 2026

Anthropic is getting caught up in the Nvidia sell-off.

By Keithen Drury – Nov 27, 2026 at 2:03AM EST

10 stocks we like better than Anthropic ›

Anthropic’s (ANT +1.08%) first two days as a publicly traded company were already days for the history books. It started off trading at about $110 per share before closing at $97. That's still a double-digit percentage gain from its list price, so it was a great day. Then it was caught up in a rout of AI stocks and, on Nov. 24, fell below its IPO price.

With a tumultuous start, Anthropic is an intriguing stock, but is it a buy? Here's where I think Anthropic could end the year priced, and investors may want to pay attention to get maximum returns.

There are multiple ways to engage in the AI investing trend. You could invest in a hardware supplier like Nvidia (NVDA -3.91%) or Broadcom (AVGO -0.07%), or in a utility company benefiting from increased electricity usage. There are also construction companies and AI hyperscalers that are putting up hundreds of billions of dollars' worth of data center infrastructure.

However, all these styles of investments have one thing in common: None of them are 100% pure-play AI investments. These companies all have other business units that aren't exposed to AI. There are very few AI-focused stocks on the market, as the entry costs to get into the AI game are quite high.

However, all that changed when Anthropic went public. Anthropic is one of the two primary AI labs (the other being OpenAI). So, with it going public first, it is drawing a lot of investor interest. Anthropic is the poster child for AI investing, as all of its business comes from the usage of its Claude family of AI models. The more AI is used, the better Anthropic does. So, if the market is bullish on AI, Anthropic will do great. If it's bearish, the market will suffer.

Right now, it's pretty clear that the market has an overall bearish stance on the AI build-out. However, that's mostly the market making its opinion on massive infrastructure spending known. There isn't much that will stop the AI hyperscalers from investing in data centers, as they see a massive opportunity.

Anthropic is a bit different from most AI companies, as it doesn't own any computing assets. Instead, it chooses to rent them. So, the risk exposure from overbuilding is practically nothing, since it only rents computing power rather than owning it. That should help it be analyzed from a pure AI usage standpoint by the market, but if market sentiment is going one way, nothing will be able to save Anthropic from further downturn.

So, what's in store for the rest of 2026?

The reality is that the AI sector is being harmed by one company: Nvidia. The resignation and return of Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang have shaken the AI industry, but aside from Nvidia, there really aren't many domino effects. Anthropic will be just fine.

I think the market will come to this realization over the next few weeks, and Anthropic stock will be bought up in December so that portfolio managers can show their clients they bought the hot new AI stock. This could contribute to Anthropic rising in a Santa Claus rally – the name of the rally that occurs during December due to heavy buying.

As a result, I wouldn't be surprised if Anthropic's stock returned to the $ 110-per-share level it opened at on Nov. 19.

On the flip side, if the market says bearish on AI, there could be more downside from here. However, I think the odds of that are lower, so I'm confident in saying Anthropic has ample upside from here.

#2910. Nets snap a five game losing streak, beat the Bucks 104-97

The Brooklyn Nets and Milwaukee Bucks came into tonight's game like two school kids trotting onto the playground, one pointing and chirping at the other "you're the worst defense," while the other bickered back "no, you're the worst defense."

Each youngster had some merit to their argument. Brooklyn had just given up 130+ in three of their last four games, and ranked dead last in defensive rating ahead of the contest. Milwaukee ranked behind them by a few fractions of a point.

Brooklyn would eventually win the squabble, and the game, but it'd take them some time to get there, not that anyone's complaining here.

Brooklyn came out the gates without a whole lot of ball pressure. In fairness, they had Danny Wolf matched up initially against Tyler Herro, which leaves you very little spatial insurance to work with. However, the Nets also looked out of sync rotating around the Milwaukee offense, which didn't help either. As Brooklyn shot 1-6 from the field and the Bucks pranced out to a 9-2 lead, it just looked like more of the same in the early going.

As I mentioned, we'd eventually find out otherwise. And while real turnaround would soon come on the defensive end, things had to start for Brooklyn at the other.

After Milwaukee's opening jolt, the Nets went on a 13-0 run in the heart of the first to go up 22-12. Michael Porter Jr., who spoke to Brain Lewis this week about how he's struggled to "find his rhythm," was in his grove amid the stretch, putting in eight first period points.

Getting five from Day'Ron Sharpe and Julius Randle each, as well as eight points off the bench in the period, the Nets led after one by a 28-24 margin.

Despite being less of a scorer this time around, Mikel Brown Jr. looked comfortable out there early on as well, starting for a second straight game. He shot 0-5 from the field in the first half but clocked in as a +4 with two assists with zero turnovers.

Also like last game, Grant Nelson joined him on the floor for a few minutes in the second. Nelson only finished with one point, but grabbed six rebounds, contributing to a strong collective effort from the team on the glass, where the Nets won 52-44. Porter Jr. also grabbed 11 rebounds, his most in a game all season if you discount the overtime affair vs Boston.

Brooklyn would need every one of those boards especially in the first half, where neither team shot it at a particularly high clip, as the Nets posted .400/.348 splits, while the Bucks had .432/.273 marks.

At the break, Brooklyn led 51-47, largely thanks to Randle, who had 16 points on 7-10 shooting from the field and 2-4 from deep. MPJ went quiet in the second, still rocking with eight points by then. Day'Ron Sharpe put us on early triple-double watch with five points, 10 rebounds, and four assists at the break.

Herro, however, had all the stats to begin the third. He poured in nine points during the period's first four minutes to put his team back in the lead for the first time since the 6:57 mark of the first. The Nets, meanwhile, opened the period 3-12 from the field and 0-5 from deep. Two of the makes came from Brown Jr, so at least there's that.

Sarcasm aside, as one young guard for Brooklyn started to turn it on, another followed. After Milwaukee looked ready to pull away, Egor Dëmin checked in, scoring five points himself and assisting on two baskets in just a minute and a half. His effort helped ignited a 13-2 run that put the lead back in Brooklyn's hands.

At the same time, Randle continued to mine inside for points. He'd eventually go for 28 in the game on 10-16 shooting. That not only gave Brooklyn consistent offense, but slowed things down and allowed them to set up easily at the other end. Even with Herro's early offensive excellence, the Bucks went 7-23 from the field in the quarter, and the Nets led going into the fourth by a 77-71 score.

"I think everybody was able to just put their minds together and not just focus on team defense, but their own island, like how we say 'guard your yard,'" Dëmin said. "I think that's where it came from. Everything else was easier because everybody was so locked in and super focused on how to guard the man in front of you rather than relying on someone else around."

And believe it or not, in that period, the defense continued to improve. On Milwaukee's second possession of the frame, Porter Jr. poked the a ball away in the backcourt, which Nelson dove across mid court to save. Still, the Bucks got it up and forward, but then Keon Ellis came charging over, intercepting the rock and then dishing it ahead Porter Jr. for an and-one finish. That was just one of the Nets seven steals in the fourth.

"Go out there and play with physicality," Sharpe said. "Go out there and play with ball pressure. Execute with staples and have each others back. I felt like we did a great job at that tonight."

Sharpe never got the triple double, but did finish 11 points, five assists, and 14 rebounds while shooting 5-10 from the field and hitting a three.

The Nets got up by 11 early in the fourth. After a few turnovers early in the frame, the Bucks cut it back down to eight, and we looked in store for a tight finish.

But in a game where Brooklyn's supposed fatal flaw had carried them, it was only right that Noah Clowney, who's struggled with his shot all year, iced things out. In the fourth, he added nine points on 4-6 shooting, scoring from all over the court to keep the lead afloat. He finished with a season-high 17 points, also grabbing five rebounds while shooting 7-13 from the field.

"This is the Noah Clowney that we need," Fernández said. "It gives us a starter off the bench and it's very, very good for this group, because it brings a player on both ends."

Dëmin, who's also had a difficult start to the year, leveraged his size to play some inspiring defense down the stretch as well, finishing as a +5 in the quarter. For the game, he had 15 points, four assists, three rebounds, a steal, and a block while shooting 5-11 from the field.

"Yeah, he went back to doing the simple things, and it started with his physicality on defense," Fernández said. "They felt him the whole game. His length. He can rebound, he can be in the gaps…Proud of him for sure."

Sure, the Nets still turned it over on back-t0-back possession with the Bucks only down 11 and under a minute to go. Dëmin missed also missed an open dunk. They're still a young, flawed team. They didn't solve all their issues tonight, but they did win the night, and snapped their five game losing streak.

Final: Brooklyn Nets 104, Milwaukee Bucks 97

Mo Wagner subbed out at the end of the first period and did not return for the rest of the game. At half, the Nets ruled him out with left knee soreness.

"I don't know. I was about to sub him in, and I saw him limping, and I was like I got to save him from himself," Fernández said. "He was there to do whatever it took for his team and his teammates, and at the end I decided to not bring him back in. Obviously, I don't know what it is, but the good thing, the great thing is he was cheering for everybody. He was cheering fro Grant [Nelson] the whole time, and that means a lot for the group, and it speaks to the type of person he is."

We'll provide updates on him once they're made available.

Day'Ron Sharpe had a season-high 14 rebounds tonight and also recorded his 5th double-double of the season with 11 points. It matches his double-double total from last season, tied as the 2nd most in his career (6, 2023-24). This was his 7th career game with 10+ PTS, 10+ REB and 5+ AST.

Noah Clowney had a season-high 17 points off the bench tonight against the Bucks. He has tied his season high with three 3-pointers made.

Michael Porter Jr. logged his third double-double of the season with 12 points and 11 rebounds. It is his 15th time recording at least 10 rebounds as a Net.

Brooklyn gets right back in action after the holiday with a road matchup against the Toronto Raptors on Friday. The North has lost seven of their last eight, including a contest to the Bucks on Monday. They're certainly looking at this one as a "get right" game. The game tips off at 7:30 p.m. EST.

#2909. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

#2907. Federal Reserve Board releases results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 10:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#2906. Diving deeper into Cavs 121-102 loss to Knicks: Even when the stars are at their best, this team isn’t

The Cleveland Cavaliers looked like they could compete with the New York Knicks until they couldn't. Cleveland went into the final quarter with a narrow 85-82 advantage, but then lost the fourth 39-17 to drop this game 121-102.

19 points on 9-of-15 shooting, 8 rebounds, 2 assists, 1 block

Bridges covered up for pretty pedestrian performances from Jalen Brunson and Karl-Anthony Towns. Bridges provided efficient scoring, was an impactful rebounder, and was good defensively. This all added up to the Knicks winning his minutes by 33.

30 points on 12-of-24 shooting, 4 rebounds, 4 assists, 4 steals, 2 blocks

Mitchell was unstoppable in the first half. His 14 points in the opening quarter and six more in the second put the Cavs in a good position to steal this game. Mitchell seemingly couldn't miss before the break, going 8-for-11 from the field with most of his damage coming at the rim.

The magic went away in the second half. Mitchell scored just 10 points on 4-of-13 shooting in the final two quarters. Cleveland then proceeded to lose those minutes by 15.

The Cavs needed a special performance from Mitchell to defeat the defending champions. They had that in the first half, but not the second.

15 points on 6-of-14 shooting, 12 assists, 7 rebounds, 3 steals, 2 blocks

Hart is a winning player. He does all the little things that championship-level teams need from their supporting cast. Rebounding, additional ball handling, defensive versatility, and ancillary playmaking are attributes you hope to accumulate in your entire supporting cast. Hart provides that as just one player.

Those skills were all on display against the Cavs. Hart rebounded well, forced turnovers, and was a useful facilitator. The Cavs have often decided to cheat off Hart offensively. He didn't make them pay as a shooter, but he used the extra space to find openings for his teammates.

On a night when both teams got similar contributions from their stars, it was the play of the supporting players, like Hart and Jose Alverado (21 points on 8-of-10 shooting), that made all the difference.

Anything bought from the links helps support Fear the Sword. You can buy the Browns throwback shirt HERE. You can also shop all of Homage's Cleveland gear HERE.

5 points on 2-of-8 shooting, 1 rebound, 0 assists

Merrill has had a tough time finding his shot this season. There's many things Merrill does well, but those are mostly muted if the outside shot isn't falling, as was once again the case here.

Head coach Kenny Atkinson has asked a lot out of Merrill this season. He's been consistently forced to guard up a spot or two when he's on the court — particularly when he's been inserted into the starting lineup. That isn't an ideal place for him considering he's undersized for that role, and has been sharing the floor with guards that don't pull their weight defensively.

I don't envy Merrill. He's been continually put into positions to fail this season.

10 points on 5-of-7 shooting, 5 rebounds, 1 assist

Allen is supposed to be a key member of this team, but you wouldn't necessarily know that from watching games like this. The Cavs need Allen to be a force inside as a roller and rebounder with Evan Mobley out of the lineup. He wasn't either of those things, and played with little to no energy, which is why the Cavs lost his minutes by 32.

The Cavs are built around their guards. If that backcourt is going to work on both ends, they need their interior players to consistently show up. They haven't, which is one of the many reasons for the underwhelming start to the season.

The Cavs benefited greatly from Bryant's energy and willingness to be physical. Both of those things stood out on a team that is in desperate need of interior size and presence. It's no coincidence the Cavs played their best basketball of the evening when he was on the court.

28 points on 7-of-19 shooting, 5 assists, 1 rebound

This was a solid night from Harden. He kept the offense moving, took smart shots, and continually forced his way to the line (13-for-14). A poor shooting night from three (1-for-8) kept this from being a great offensive game.

Defensively, the Cavs had some success with hiding him on Towns. Harden held his own in that matchup, resulting in the defense not completely falling apart with him on the floor.

Harden did a good job of forcing his way inside. The offense is in a much better place when he can consistently do that.

Jose Alverado scored as many points off the bench (21) as all five of Cleveland's reserves combined.

This was a bad night from Cleveland's supporting cast, particularly their bench. Tyrese Proctor had just two points on 1-of-6 shooting, Jaylon Tyson went 2-for-8 from the field with four turnovers, and Mario Hezonja registered no points in 10 minutes.

Conversely, New York got key contributions from their role players, including Alverado. He scored 16 of his 21 points in the fourth to help put the Cavs away.

This team makes much more sense on paper than it does on the court. The Cavs have more well-rounded role players than they have had in recent years. This has shown through when they get opportunities to shine. Yet, that may not be what works with this particular core.

Typically, the Cavs are at their best when Mitchell and Harden are playing well, as they were on Wednesday. This formula has been the recipe for their top performances, but the Cavs didn't come close to pulling off the upset because they got next to no help from their supporting cast. No player besides the starting backcourt scored more than 10 points. It's nearly impossible to win games against an elite opponent when your role players add nothing.

Somehow, the Cavs have constructed a team that relies heavily on their guards to be at their best to win alongside a supporting cast that doesn't really fit with how the guards play. That is a problem, and how you get nights like this where Peyton Watson and Mario Hezonja combine to take just five shots from the field.

The Cavs are pot committed to this backcourt both in terms of their recent contract extensions and how they play. Atkinson hasn't indicated that he wants to move away from this isolation- and high pick-and-roll-heavy offense that caters specifically to the two guards. But that style isn't conducive to a team that has struggled to knock down catch-and-shoot threes (36.3%, 16th in the league), doesn't have a versatile defense, and features two bigs that need to be force-fed to be effective offensively.

Koby Altman has built an interesting supporting cast. On paper, it would fit nicely with a team that played like the 2024-25 Cavs. However, this isn't that team. Any hopes of playing that way went out the window when they traded for Harden last winter.

Cleveland has been one of the most inconsistent groups this season. Few teams have as impressive highs and as disappointing lows. Many factors contribute to that, but one of them is that the puzzle pieces don't really fit together. Or at the very least, they don't fit with how they're being used now.

There's no easy fix for this. Getting a team to drastically change how they play 19 games into the season is nearly impossible. At the same time, there isn't an easy way to pivot off either the star or supporting players due to their contracts. It's difficult to see a trade drastically changing the outlook of this team. They're more or less committed to this current group and playstyle. And I'm just not sure what the pathway is to being a legit title contender, which is what you would hope they'd be coming off a run to the Eastern Conference Finals.

Even when the stars are at or near their best, this team isn't.

#2904. Dillon Brooks gets ejected with 23 seconds left vs. Rockets after getting into it with Kevin Durant

Phoenix Suns guard Dillon Brooks and Houston Rockets forward Kevin Durant exchanged words at the end of Houston's 116-104 win on Wednesday night.

Durant was chippy all night, making sure to deliver extra trash talk against his former team.

With less than 30 seconds left in the game, the Rockets led by 10. In a wild moment, Durant and Brooks weren't really playing; they were just talking to each other near halfcourt while the action continued.

Houston's Fred VanVleet hit a jumper with 23.8 seconds left to make it a 114-102 game in the Rockets' favor. Brooks and Durant were chirping at each other and got in each other's faces.

The referees assessed each player a technical foul. Because Brooks had received a technical foul earlier in the quarter with 4:32 left, he was ejected. VanVleet drained a three and motioned to Brooks; they chirped and were both assessed technicals.

Durant finished the game with 20 points and 10 rebounds. With 46.3 seconds left in the second quarter, the veteran forward drained a 3-pointer. That basket helped him become the fifth player in NBA history to score at least 33,000 points. He joins LeBron James (43,672 and counting), Kareem Abdul-Jabbar (38,387), Karl Malone (36,298) and Kobe Bryant (33,643).

The win moved Houston to 14-5. The Rockets return to action Saturday, hosting the Philadelphia 76ers.

The loss dropped Phoenix to 9-8. The Suns had won four straight before Wednesday's loss, and now turn their attention to taking on the Dallas Mavericks on Friday night.

#2902. Federal Reserve issues FOMC statement

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 2:00 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

#2900. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.

Koontz ranks No. 357 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 42% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.7%. See SpaceX Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2895. Game Recap: Suns run out of fuel in 116-104 loss to Rockets

The Phoenix Suns fought valiantly, but couldn't finish the road trip undefeated as the Houston Rockets held off a late push by the Suns and earned a 116-104 victory. Phoenix clawed within two at 99-97, but the Rockets finished on a 17-7 run.

Alperen Sengun finished the game with 23 points, 11 rebounds and 11 assists, recording his third triple-double of the season. Seven of those rebounds were on the offensive glass. Steven Adams also snatched six offensive boards, and the Rockets won the overall rebounding battle 51-38.

Oso Ighodaro had 13 points on 6-for-8 shooting, but managed just two rebounds in 20 minutes before fouling out. Khaman Maluach added 12 points of his own and made several outstanding plays, but is still a little too raw to compete with the burly Adams and All-Star Sengun.

The Suns were an abysmal 5-for-32 from three-point range in the game. Jordan Goodwin was 2-for-4, which made the rest of the team 3-for-28. That looks like a typo. Posting a -13 on the boards and shooting 15.6% from the three-point range makes it hard to win a basketball game. A bad shooting night is one thing, but the rebounding situation is starting to look like a fatal flaw for this squad.

Miles Bridges struggled through his worst offensive game of the season, recording just two points while shooting 0-6 from three-point range and 1-8 overall. Devin Booker had a game-high 25 points and Jalen Green chipped in 16, but they were 6-18 and 8-21 from the field, respectively. Booker did knock down 11-of-12 free throws to continue his impressive performance from the line this season.

The Suns' bench group played well overall. In addition to Maluach's performance, Goodwin scored 11 points and grabbed five rebounds, but played just 17 minutes. Kevin Durant recorded a double-double with 20 points and 10 rebounds, while Amen Thompson recorded his own with 18 and 10. That is two more than the Suns had.

The Rockets' starters were just too much for Phoenix to contend with tonight. Plain and simple.

The Suns' aspirations of advancing to the next round in the NBA Cup have been quashed with this loss.

Phoenix will try to get back in the win column when they host the Dallas Mavericks and Cooper Flagg at 7:00pm Friday.

The Rockets raced out to an 11-4 lead with Kevin Durant getting five quick points. The Suns recovered to tie it up at 11, and for a long stretch in the quarter it looked like we might be watching a defense-optional game.

KD kept pouring it in and already had 11 points when the Rockets went up 22-18. At that time, the Rockets held a 10-2 free-throw disparity over the Suns.

The Suns were trying to play physical, but the referees didn't let them impose their will, opting to let Durant and Adams flop to get calls. Maluach ended up with a fairly weak flagrant one as he somehow managed to plant Adams face first on the ground. Or maybe Adams just laid down that way on purpose.

Jordan Goodwin entered and hit a quick three-pointer to pull the Suns within one at 23-22. Goodwin is shooting a career-high 38.6% this season from three-point range.

A dunk and damage for a three-point play from Maluach tied the game at 25. Khaman was playing very well overall but still displayed hand problems during the game. Goodwin hit his second three-pointer to give the Suns a 32-27 lead courtesy of a 10-2 run. The first quarter ended 33-29 in the Suns' favor. Booker finished the quarter with 13 points and survived a massive block by Amen Thompson.

Goodwin was fouled on a three-point attempt after making his first two. He sank all three free throws to give the Suns their biggest lead of the game at 38-31. The Oso/Kennard/Gillespie/Brooks/Goodwin lineup meshed well together, but the Rockets started punishing them inside.

Steven Adams grabbed four quick offensive rebounds. There just isn't a player on the Suns with the size and strength of Adams.

He helped the Rockets go on a 13-7 run to cut the Suns' lead to 45-44. Maluach was still making his presence felt. A few plays after a block on Sengun, he nailed a jumper as the shot clock expired. In between that, he mishandled the ball on offense for a turnover. Such are the highs and lows with the 20-year-old.

Sengun was still winning the matchup overall. After another offensive rebound, Sengun hit a three to cap an 11-2 run that put Houston up 57-51. That turned into a 20-7 run by the time the quarter was over, and Houston led 66-56 at the half. The Suns had squandered their early lead and would trail the remainder of the game.

Booker finished the first half with 15 points and four assists, while Goodwin chipped in nine points.

The Rockets starters had a well-balanced attack. Durant scored 14, Sengun had 13 along with seven rebounds and six assists, Jabari Smith Jr. poured in 12, and Amen just missed double figures with nine points. Houston led the battle of the boards 27-15, including 12-4 on the offensive end. The Rockets shot 26 free throws, but made just 14, while the Suns were 11-14 from the charity stripe.

Sengun, Durant and Amen Thompson were back to mangling the Suns as Sengun inched closer to a triple-double. The Rockets pushed their lead to a game-high 14 as they took a 79-65 lead. Phoenix fought back to cut it to six at 79-73, but a three-pointer by Tari Eason pushed it right back to 12.

The Suns were 0-6 from three-point range in the third. They hung around but couldn't get closer than six points. Lapses like the shot clock violation on their last possession in the quarter seemed to pop up and prevent them from completely catching up.

Maluach and Goodwin finished the quarter with 11 points each. Houston led 92-81.

Luke Kennard was awarded a technical free throw to start the 4th quarter on a positive note. Oso followed that up with a layup and was fouled but missed the free throw. Then it was Gillespie with the bump and the bucket, but he converted his three-point play. Suddenly it was 92-87, and the Suns were right back in it.

The teams traded a few buckets before Kennard cut the lead to four at 99-95. Green blew past a defender for a layup. 99-97. VanVleet drained a dagger three to make it 104-98 after Maluach went 1-for-2 from the line.

Sengun dished out an assist with 3:49 left to notch his third triple-double of the season. Booker made a layup to help the Suns keep pace at 106-100, but after a defensive stop both Booker and Brooks missed wide-open looks. Jabari Smith Jr. missed the second of two free throws, but Sengun scored off another offensive rebound to push the lead to 109-100. A VanVleet three-pointer made it 112-102.

The game ended with Brooks and KD in each other's face. Dillon got to start his shower 23.8 seconds early. I was frustrated too. The Rockets held on for a 116-104 victory, evening the season series 1-1.

It's Dallas on Friday. We'll see you then!
