#2885. San Antonio at Portland, Final Score: the Spurs lose their first road game 116-111 to the Blazers

The Spurs hadn't lost a road game yet this season, but that came to an end tonight as Damian Lillard with 29 points and Deni Avidja with 33 rallied Rip City to a 5 point win in a game that was a back and forth contest all night. Former Spur Sidy Cissoko had 16 points and made some key plays down the stretch as Victor Wembanyama's 44 points weren't enough to get the Silver and Black to a win. The Spurs shot poorly from three, but the bigger problem was lack of attention to detail, as some critical mistakes and missed free throws down the stretch were the deciding factor, as the Blazers thrilled the home crowd and pulled out a big NBA Cup win. The Spurs will have to win their game on Friday to have a shot at advancing the the knockout rounds in the Cup tournament. They have a pretty solid point differential, so even if they don't win the group, they could make it into the tournament as a wildcard. But first they have to win on Friday. Go Spurs, BEAT LA!

The lead-in game was Thunder vs Timberwolves, and the Wolves won 112-106, but mostly I was distracted by Chet Holmgren wearing a fur coat on the bench.

Dame was pretty much held in control in the first meeting, but tonight, he started out hot, scoring the first 7 for the Blazers. When he hit a logo triple at about four minutes in, Mitch had to call a quick TO. Wemby sat after the TO, taking a very short first rotation of just 4 minutes.

The Spurs bench did a decent job of holding back the Blazers until Wemby returned to the lineup with 3 minutes left in the quarter.

The Spurs attempted 15 free throws in the first quarter to 10 for Portland, which kept them in the game, as they trailed 35-38 after the first. The bench squad held up pretty well, with Jordan McLaughlin having some good minutes showing his quickness and ability to get to the rim.

Ja'Kobi Gillespie got into the game at the start of the second quarter, which is a good sign that he's already gaining Mitch Johnson's trust as a viable backup.

Advija is pretty adept at using his off arm to get around defenders. It's an offensive foul, but I guess if nobody is going to call it, it's a good move.

In their first meeting on November 7, the Spurs held Deni Avdija to 15 points. He scored 18 in the first half tonight. They held Lillard to 12, and he had an 18 point first half. This was going to be a tougher go for the Silver and Black, but they only trailed by three at the half, with the score at 60 Blazers, 57 Spurs.

The Blazers tried to single cover Wembanyama with Clingan. It didn't work out well for them.

How many time have we seen Wembanyama turn an ankle, and somehow be just fine after going to the floor for a minute or two. His physical resilience is superhuman.

The Spurs kept pace for most of the third quarter but a stretch of wacky ball about two thirds through the frame let the Blazers get some easy buckets as they took an 82-77 lead. Mitch called a timeout, and the Spurs put Wemby back in the game leading to a 7-0 Spurs run as they retook the lead. After a couple of Dame free throws, a Devin Vassell buzzer beater at the end of the quarter gave the Spur an 87-84 lead going into the fourth.

The Blazers had an offensive explosion to start the fourth quarter, with Dame hitting a deep three over Wembanyama and Sidy Cissoko making an impressive dunk on a drive to the bucket, and the home team was suddenly up 96-89, their biggest lead of the game.

Victor can't play the whole game, and the bench had to take a turn, but they actually held their own, and by the time that Wemanyama came in at 5:43, the Blazer lead was only 3 points, the bench had done their part, and it was Wemby's job to bring it home.

Clingan fouled out with 4 minutes and earned a tech for complaining about it. Sadly, the Silver and Black could not cash the free throw, and with a lane violation of one of Wemby's free throws, they left two points wanting, which could be costly.

The Blazers caused an 8 second call on Castle bringing up the ball, and after a few empty trips, a crazy banking three point shot from Avdija gave Portland a 5 point lead with under a minute left. After another empty trip by the Silver and Black, the Blazers had the ball and called timeout. They just had to get the ball in bounds and they had it in the bag, unless they turn it over on the inbounds play, which they did. The Spurs cut the lead to one score with 17 seconds left, but the Blazers were done giving gifts to the Spurs, and they closed it out with free throws, winning 116-111.

For the opponents' viewpoint, visit Blazer's Edge.

#2882. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.â¯â¯

20th Street and Constitution Avenue N.W., Washington, DC 20551

#2880. Cowboys vs. Eagles recap: Dallas embarrassed by Philadelphia on Thanksgiving Day

It will go down in history as one of the darkest Thanksgiving Day games ever for the Dallas Cowboys.

Somehow, some way, the Cowboys threw away a 31-10 lead that they had in the third quarter.

The Philadelphia Eagles won on what was basically a game-winning, 60-yard field goal. It is hard to fathom.

On the other hand it is actually very easy to fathom given everything we have seen from the Cowboys this season. They are not a good football team. They are poorly coached. They are talent-deficient. They are all of the bad things.

On a day where we are supposed to be thankful it is hard to find a reason to be for this specific part of our lives. The Dallas Cowboys are a weekly embarrassment and Thursday was the latest example of it.

The Eagles won the toss and deferred which put the Cowboys offense to work first.

Dak Prescott and the group did not waste the opportunity. Javonte Williams was served seconds, thirds, and then some as the team marched down the field, partly thanks to a huge 3rd and 18 conversion from Dak to CeeDee Lamb, but it was Williams who capped it off with the touchdown to give the home team an early 7-0 lead.

Things were looking rather impressive for Joe Milton and the Eagles. I'd imagine that many Cowboys fans were starting to assume a score was happening any minute across the first drive.

Thankfully that was not the case. Right around field goal range the Eagles chose to roll the dice on fourth and short… and Quinnen Williams said no!

Weirdly a sequence occurred on the next drive that did the first.

Dak Prescott scrambled for a first on a third down, but the gain was negated by a penalty. This brought up third and long (16 here, 18 before) and Prescott connected with a star receiver (George Pickens here, CeeDee Lamb before) for the first down.

After that KaVontae Turpin had a stellar catch where he turned on the boosters and reached the endzone in a flash. Dallas was up 14-0 early.

The quarter ended with Philly holding the ball right after.

The Eagles punted shortly after teams flipped the field which put the Cowboys offense back up. Given that they were already leading 14-0 this was a chance to really blow things open. Unfortunately they could not and wound up punting the ball for the first time in a while.

A big 3rd and 12 conversion from Philadelphia put them within striking distance and while they were unable to fully get home they did give Jake Elliott a chance at a 48-yard field goal. It was good and the visitors had their first points on the day.

The Cowboys offense did not miss this time around. KaVontae Turpin was involved again, this time on a jet sweep, and there was a big conversion from Dak Prescott to CeeDee Lamb.

But it was Javonte Williams who cased in yet again. This marked his second score on the day and gave Dallas a 21-3 lead.

Joe Milton had been showing moments of promise to this point in the game and at long last he delivered. It was a classic Joe Milton throw… meaning it traveled quite a long ways.

The Cowboys received the ball with just over four minutes to go. It felt like an important possession as it would likely be their last one before the half and they received the ball to start the game. Points were essential.

In all honesty it was a pretty penalty-filled drive. Officials were calling them left and right, many of them helping the Cowboys, but Dallas was moved back after an apparent touchdown to Brevyn Spann-Ford.

On third and goal from the 12-yard line… Dak Prescott found Javonte Williams for a score to close the half. It also marked a hat trick of scores on the day for Williams specifically.

The Eagles took a knee and it was Miranda Lambert time.

Philadelphia went three and out to start the second half which is always great to see. The Cowboys took the field and once again with an opportunity to stretch their lead.

Dallas punted it back themselves. This game truly felt over, but obviously with a lot of time left that was not the case in any real way. Football had to be played.

Shemar James sacked Joe Milton which helped to ensure the punt streak continued. Philly booted it back and the Cowboys offense returned to the field.

It was a little bit of the same for the Cowboys offense in terms of not being able to fully convert. They did score, but only 3 points by way of Brandon Aubrey. This was notable though as it was a 59-yard field goal that he drilled with ease which was great to see given how this season has gone. The FOX broadcast also noted that this was the longest ever field goal made on Thanksgiving all-time. Pretty neat.

The third quarter ended right as Philadelphia crossed the 50-yard line… and then Joe Milton found DeVonta Smith again to get another touchdown on the board with 15 seconds left. It was suddenly 31-17, good guys.

Things did not go particularly well for the Cowboys offense to start the fourth quarter. They faced a fourth and short on their side of the field and left the offense on the field. It was bold, but nobody really thought they were going to run a play. Thankfully they just fished for offsides and punted.

Or so we thought. The Cowboys tried to punt… but it was blocked and returned for a Philly touchdown. Out of nowhere it was 31-24 with basically the entire fourth quarter left.

It goes without saying that the pressure was high. The Cowboys were on the verge of blowing this game. Something had to happen.

Dak Prescott and CeeDee Lamb specifically played like that was the case. They hooked up for a pair of big gains along the drive and then Dak put the exclamation point on things himself by running it on for a touchdown… thanks to some serious hops.

It seemed like this was going to essentially end the game. Have you seen the 2026 Dallas Cowboys, though

Philadelphia traveled 42 yards in 3 plays to match the touchdown. Joe Milton literally did by rushing it in himself.

The Eagles missed the two-point conversion so it was 38-30 Cowboys. In the blink of an eye it was 3rd and 14 right past the team's own 15-yard line.

It appeared as if the Cowboys had pulled off something truly incredible. Dak Prescott found George Pickens for a modest gain, but then he threw a lateral across the field to Hunter Luepke and the fullback took it all the way to the house. He even jumped into the Salvation Army kettle.

Unfortunately it was deemed to be a forward pass from Pickens and therefore a penalty which resulted in the loss of the down. Bryan Anger successfully got the punt off this time and the Eagles returned it past midfield. There was still half the fourth quarter remaining.

It was then that the Cowboys defense made a play. On third and forever they picked off Joe Milton. Cobie Durant was the hero in question.

You would think that this would have given the Cowboys the opportunity to ice the game. You would think that the offense that had been on fire for so long would have scored to fully put things away.

They wound up settling for a field goal attempt from 40 yards out. Brandon Aubrey missed. It was unbelievable.

Fine. Mistakes happen. Surely the Cowboys defense would lock in.

It took the Eagles just 4 plays to travel 70 yards and score. Seriously.

Given the failed two-point conversion earlier though the Eagles had to have one here. They failed again so Dallas retained a slim lead at 38-36.

The Cowboys got past midfield. In fact, they got all the way to the Philadelphia 39-yard line.

They were facing 4th and 1. Instead of letting Brandon Aubrey try a field goal, he had just missed obviously, the Cowboys chose to go for it. Dak Prescott was sacked. It felt grim.

You know what happened. The Eagles pulled off a 60-yard field goal to win 39-38.

The Cowboys' season is over. Unbelievable.

#2878. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guide for Traditional Community Banking Organizations

#2877. Rockets Scurry Over Suns To Another 116-104 Victory

Another game, another 12pt victory that never felt comfortable, but never seemed much in doubt, either. While the previous opponent, the LA Clippers are not a good team, the Suns are good, or at least decent. They beat the Rockets in Phoenix not that long ago, in a game that has been described as strange, or one where the Rockets seemed asleep.

Because I've been dealing with an household electrical problem (that took out the refrigerator) which, of course, cropped up on Thanksgiving Eve; this might be a shorter recap. (I fixed the problem BTW, Thanksgiving is saved, hooray for me.) It's also a shorter recap because it's hard to pin much of a narrative on the past two Rockets game. They look like easy wins, but weren't. The Rockets didn't play their best, but won. The defense didn't seem great, but was. No one player stood out as the star, or the goat, of the team. The offense didn't look great, but it was above NBA average.

In both of the games, Clippers and Suns, it seemed as though the Rockets knew they were more talented than their opponent, turned on the afterburners for a short time, and then just played keep away.

Here's what I mean – quarter by quarter scoring tonight:

The Rockets scored a lot for the first half, then just kind of added slightly to a lead that spent the time from around the middle of the second quarter onward fluctuating between about 10 to 6 points.

The Suns obviously came out in this game with a plan to play physically with the Rockets. This lead to a whopping 26 free throws in the first half for the Rockets. In troubling news, the Rockets made only 14 of those 26 free throws, or this could have been a substantially more comfortable win. Sengun went 2-6, Thompson 4-9, Jabari 3-5, though Durant was more consistent, at 5-6. Where the good free throw shooting from the earliest part of the season has gone is anyone's guess. It hasn't cost the Rockets as it did in Denver, but neither the Suns nor the Clippers are a strong a team as the Nuggets. If it doesn't improve, though, it might cost them as soon as Saturday, when the Rockets face the 15-4 Philadelphia 76ers.

The Suns changed tack in the second half, apparently, and the Rockets only shot six more free throws in the second half.

The Rockets continue not to have a single fulcrum in terms of scoring or usage, but it's clear the engine remains Alperen Sengun. Sengun is averaging 22pts/11rbs/7ast/1stl/1blk/3TO on 55%FG shooting, 63TS%, 50%3pt on 26% usage in 33mpg. If you asked most NBA fans who was playing better, Sengun or Luka Doncic, they'd all say Doncic. But Doncic is shooting worse overall at 46%, his True Shooting Percentage is lower, despite him taking over 10FT per game, his assists are about 3/4 of an assist higher, than Sengun, his rebounds 4 lower, and his usage is sitting at a staggering 41% compared to Sengun's 26%. The argument can be made that Doncic's gaudy points total is largely driven by usage, while Sengun is the more effective player. Yes, Doncic is a heliocentric player, so perhaps he gets less help (perhaps), but that's by design.

Anyhow, Sengun has been fantastic, those are excellent numbers, and his effect is similarly profound for the Rockets. He remains, despite everything, 24 1/3 years old. He's also making 50% of his threes.

At some point we will have to have a serious conversation about Ime Udoka's truest soulmate, Marcus Smart. He's an intense player, and an incredibly streaky shooter. He also can simply lose his mind in a game, and throw a punch, or beef with a fan, or any of a variety of undesirable things. Tonight he almost single-handedly lead a Phoenix comeback tonight by committing a shooting foul on three consecutive plays before he was thankfully removed. Were they all fouls? Pretty much, yes.

We also got a waning seconds, late game stare down standoff, between Dillon Brooks and Kevin Durant. It looked for an instant like Durant was contemplating throwing a punch. If he was Marcus Smart, he probably would have done it. Instead he settled for an unnecessary dunk with seconds remaining. This marked another good game for Durant with 20pts on 7-15 shooting but a woeful 1-7 from three. Extrapolating from that he was almost perfect with his mid range shot, and it looking exactly as great as 7-8.

The next highest scorer was Amen Thompson at 18 points, and it could have been better if he made more free throws. Amen has returned to form, and he had a lot of space to work with near the rim tonight as the Suns seemed extremely focused on Sengun.

The Rockets looked like they might give this one away in the fourth quarter, but the man the Rockets missed last season, Fred VanVleet did indeed ride to the rescue, steadied the ship, toed the line, and took the wheel. So for a second game things might look a bit better on the scoreboard than the Rockets played. At least their mental fragility didn't wreck them. Dillon Brooks would not have let them hear the end of it.

The Rockets and Suns overall played a pretty feisty, chippy game with a lot of the player chosen with the Rockets pick last year Khaman Maluach trying to molest Alperen Sengun in a basketball sense. This was not successful, but he's a powerful player with a nice close shooting touch who might one day be excellent. Today was not that day.

Tomorrow, though, is Thanksgiving! On behalf of TDS, happy Thanksgiving to all who celebrate, and our best wishes to those who don't.

#2873. Eagles pull off miracle Thanksgiving rally as Cowboys stunningly collapse after disastrous decision

ARLINGTON, Texas — Brian Schottenheimer said he second-guesses plenty of his decisions, just not the one that will be dissected more than any other in his two seasons as coach of the Dallas Cowboys.

Jake Elliott kicked a 59-yard field goal with 2 seconds remaining, and the Philadelphia Eagles stunned the Cowboys by rallying for a 39-38 Thanksgiving victory on Thursday.

The Eagles (4-7) got a chance for the win when Schottenheimer decided to go for it with a two-point lead on fourth-and-1 from the Philadelphia 39-yard line with 55 seconds remaining. Dak Prescott was sacked at the 48 of the Eagles, who needed just 11 yards to give Elliott a shot at the long field goal with 7 seconds to go.

A punt could have run off about 10 seconds and pinned the Eagles deep in their end of the field with no timeouts.

The shocking loss is sure to turn up the pressure on Schottenheimer after the Cowboys (5-7) again failed to win consecutive games and made any path to the playoffs difficult at best.

"Absolutely not in that situation," Schottenheimer said when asked if he second-guessed the decision. "There's things that come up and you look at the film and you want to know, 'What do you think about that?' But that situation, absolutely not."

Philadelphia quarterback Joe Milton runs in for a touchdown during the Eagles 39-38 comeback win over the Cowboys on Nov. 26, 2026, in Arlington, Texas. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

The Cowboys have one of the worst defenses in the league, and let Joe Milton, a quarterback who couldn't win the backup job behind Prescott in training camp this year, throw for 331 yards in just his third career start. All three have been with the Eagles since he was signed off the Dallas practice squad last month.

Prescott is taking his responsibility, saying he didn't communicate a change at the line of scrimmage and then hesitated on a throw to CeeDee Lamb before Ty Robinson closed in for the sack.

"That's one I'll think about for a long time," Prescott said. "Still had a chance at the throw, just so the DB was playing CeeDee pretty physical. And then I couldn't get off of that, felt the pressure, and took the sack. And I can't do that. Most important part of the game."

Dallas' only winning streak in two seasons with Schottenheimer was a three-game run last November capped by a Thanksgiving win over Patrick Mahomes and Kansas City.

The Cowboys led by 21 points in the third quarter — the biggest blown lead in Thanksgiving history — and were still up 14 in the fourth before the Eagles started their improbable comeback by blocking a punt and returning it for a touchdown.

Milton had touchdown passes of 50 and 45 yards to DeVonta Smith and a 14-yard scoring run as the Eagles won when they were already missing quarterback Jalen Hurts and lost star running back Saquon Barkley (stinger) and cornerback Cooper DeJean (concussion) in the first quarter.

The defending NFC East champions have won back-to-back games since a seven-game losing streak.

"A lot of emotions, but mainly just so happy for this team," said Milton, who was Prescott's backup last year before losing the job to Sam Howell this preseason. "This team never let up. These guys on the sideline, they never dropped their head. Everybody just kept saying, 'We're good, we're good. We're gonna keep going.'"

Jake Elliott celebrates after hitting the game-winning, 59-yard field goal in the Eagles' 39-38 come-from-behind victory over the Cowboys on Nov. 26, 2026, in Arlington, Texas. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

Prescott threw for two touchdowns and ran for a score while Javonte Williams accounted for three TDs in a game the Cowboys didn't trail until Elliott's winning kick.

The Cowboys were up 14 early in the fourth when Robinson blocked Bryan Anger's punt, and Kenny Moore II picked up the bouncing ball at the Dallas 3 for the easy score.

Prescott answered by running through and vaulting over Moore on a 14-yard run that restored the two-touchdown lead.

Dallas then had another special teams breakdown on Britain Covey's 55-yard kickoff return. Smith, who finished with a career-high 200 yards receiving, had a 23-yard catch after having gone to the sideline earlier with an apparent back injury when he landed hard on an incompletion. Milton ran 15 yards for a score on the next play.

Defensive tackle Ty Robinson (95) blocks Bryan Anger's punt during the fourth quarter of the Eagles' comeback road win over the Cowboys. AP Photo/Julio Cortez

The Eagles went for a 2-point conversion and didn't get it, so they had to try again after Makai Lemon's 24-yard TD catch pulled them within 38-36 with 2:10 remaining. After a holding penalty, Milton's pass was broken up at the goal line.

Before Lemon's score, Brandon Aubrey missed a 40-yard field goal that would have put Dallas up 11.

Milton was 21 of 31 with three touchdowns in his third career start. The 50-yarder to Smith marked the fifth consecutive week Dallas has given up a touchdown at least that long.

Prescott's TD passes were to KaVontae Turpin and Williams, who also had touchdown runs of 1 and 12 yards. Lamb had seven catches for 134 yards.

Dallas set an NFL record with a fourth home loss this season when scoring at least 30 points. The Cowboys are the first in league history to average at least 30 points through 12 games (31.3) and have a losing record.

The Cowboys started November by matching a franchise record with a blown fourth-quarter lead of 14 points in a 33-30 overtime loss to Arizona. They ended the month with a replay against the Eagles, who tied the biggest fourth-quarter comeback on Thanksgiving. Chicago beat Detroit 23-17 in overtime in 1980.

"I guess I said a couple of weeks ago after the Arizona game how stunning that one was," Prescott said. "But this definitely takes the cake."

#2872. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Democrats' midterm election win has put the artificial intelligence trade in question, as voters clearly expressed opposition to the rapid buildout of data centers. The stock market could remain volatile amid AI trade concerns, macro uncertainties, and geopolitical tensions.

In this scenario, investors can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios to earn steady income. However, the extensive universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as these experts assign ratings after in-depth financial analysis.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Viper Energy (VNOM) is a subsidiary of Diamondback and owns and acquires mineral and royalty interests, primarily in the Permian Basin. The company reported upbeat third-quarter earnings, supported by recent acquisitions and continued development activity from Diamondback and third-party operators. Viper announced a base cash dividend of 50 cents per share for Q3 2026, payable on Nov. 19. Based on the base and variable dividends announced over the past 12 months, VNOM's dividend yield stands at 5.7%.

Following the Q3 results, TD Cowen analyst Aaron Bilkoski reiterated a buy rating on Viper Energy stock with a price target of $61. The analyst noted that the company delivered solid Q3 results, with production, including oil and natural gas liquids, exceeding both TD Cowen and consensus estimates.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that the key update was the announcement of two royalty acquisitions for $809 million in cash, including deferred consideration, and $82 million of newly issued shares.

Given the recently announced base dividend hike, Bilkoski expects Viper to direct incremental free cash flow toward buybacks. While the acquisitions increase leverage, the analyst believes that Viper has enough capacity for opportunistic buybacks.

Bilkoski noted that Viper has historically delivered one of the highest production per share growth profiles in his royalty coverage, but still trades at a notable discount to the royalty peer average and the company's historical FCF yield range. "In our view, its above-average growth warrants a higher valuation," said the analyst.

Bilkoski ranks No. 525 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.80%. See Viper Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Exxon Mobil (XOM) reported robust Q3 2026 profits, as its U.S. refineries boosted production to capitalize on the high diesel fuel prices sparked by the conflict in the Middle East. The oil and gas giant announced a quarterly dividend of $1.07 per share, payable on Dec. 10, reflecting a 3.9% increase. This marks Exxon's 44th consecutive year of dividend increases. At an annualized dividend of $4.28 per share, XOM offers a yield of 2.53%.

Impressed by the Q3 print, TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Exxon stock and raised his price target to $193 from $180 to reflect higher downstream earnings, with the expectation of increased mid-cycle margins.

Gabelman noted that the strength in Exxon's refining business continues to offset Middle East-related headwinds. He added that the company increased its ownership in LNG projects, which should reduce its Middle East concentration over time from 75% to 50% and lower the likelihood of diversification-focused mergers and acquisitions. Beyond LNG, Gabelman expects Exxon's growth in the 2030s to come from Guyana, Kashagan, Permian, and possibly Venezuela.

Gabelman noted that Exxon announced a dividend hike of nearly 4% and plans to maintain buybacks at $20 billion for 2027. "The decision to maintain reduces the pro-cyclicality nature of the buyback," said the analyst.

Gabelman ranks No. 347 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.90%. See Exxon Mobil Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Williams (WMB), which owns and operates energy infrastructure to deliver natural gas, reported strong third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of $0.525 per share, or $2.10 per share annualized, payable on Dec. 28. WMB offers a dividend yield of 3.09%.

In reaction to the Q3 2026 results, CIBC analyst Robert Catellier reaffirmed a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $82. The analyst noted that Q3 results were essentially in line with expectations and reflected continued strength in execution across WMB's infrastructure portfolio.

Catellier said that Williams' Q3 performance was driven by higher transmission, storage, and power generation revenues, as well as contributions from the Momentum Midstream acquisition.

He noted that Williams maintained its full-year EBITDA guidance of $8.3 billion to $8.5 billion. In fact, results are tracking toward the higher end of the guidance range, backed by contributions from recently completed projects and the Momentum Midstream deal. WMB continues to target long-term EBITDA compound annual growth rate of at least 11% through 2030.

Catellier expects WMB's long-term targets to be supported by incremental transmission and storage opportunities, including Transco's Southeast Supply Enhancement II project and an 84 MMcf/d expansion of the Garden Connector project.

Catellier ranks No. 667 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 9.60%. See Williams Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2870. Spurs drop NBA Cup game to Trail Blazers despite Wembanyama’s big game

It felt like a playoff game in the Moda Center on the night before Thanksgiving. Maybe it was the drive to win an NBA Cup game, or maybe it was the Portland Trail Blazers wanting revenge on the San Antonio Spurs from last year's playoffs, and a thumping earlier this season. Either way, the Blazers were motivated, and the crowd was raucous. The Spurs kept pace for most of the game, but faltered late in a 116-111 loss.

It looked like Victor Wembanyama would will the Spurs to victory without De'Aaron Fox and Dylan Harper, who missed the game with injuries. Wembanyama had it going in the post, scoring 44 points on 17-for-30 shooting. Immediately, he took Blazers center Donovan Clingan into the low post, scoring over him or forcing him to foul, eventually leading to Clingan fouling out.

However, Wembanyama's effort wasn't enough to overcome Portland getting stellar performances from two of their stars. Damian Lillard looked like prime Dame, hitting three after three over the Spurs defenders. Lillard had 29 points and 6 assists while shooting 5-of-12 from three. He was outshined by his teammate, Deni Avdija, who hit the big bank three at the end of the game to seal the win for Portland. He had 33 points, 12 rebounds, and 7 assists on an efficient 11-of-21 shooting from the field.

San Antonio came into the fourth quarter seemingly taking control. A Devin Vassell fadeaway three-pointer to beat the third-quarter buzzer put the team up three points. After struggling to shoot the ball in the first half, the team got it going in the third quarter, shooting 6-of-10 from three-point range. With Portland out of challenges and its big man in foul trouble, the Spurs had several advantages to steal a win on the road.

Immediately, the Blazers went on a 12-2 run to take a 7-point lead early in the fourth quarter. They played with a lot of force on both ends. That was particularly clear when Sidy Cissoko drove right into Wembanyama and dunked over the top of him. The former Spur had 16 points, knocking down four of his ten three-pointers.

All game long, but especially down the stretch, the Blazers were able to get into the Spurs' ball-handlers' space and force mistakes. San Antonio turned the ball over 14 times, giving up 14 points off turnovers. That defensive pressure especially hurt the Spurs in the final two minutes of the game, when San Antonio turned the ball over two possessions in a row with the chance to tie the game.

Stephon Castle took on the majority of the ball-handling responsibilities and had an up-and-down game. Castle finished with 20 points, 9 assists, 6 rebounds, and 3 turnovers while shooting 6-of-18 from the field. He was stopped in the backcourt by Toumani Camara for an 8-second violation late in the game that gave the Blazers a shot of momentum heading into the end of the game.

Overall, it was hard for the Spurs to find an offensive rhythm outside of forcing the ball to Wembanyama in the high or low post. San Antonio shot just 43% from the field and 28% from three.

With the loss, the Spurs fall to 2-1 in the Western Conference Group C. They are in a four-way tie for first with Portland, the Golden State Warriors, and the Los Angeles Lakers. The Spurs play the Lakers, and the Blazers play the Warriors on Friday. San Antonio needs to win that game, and Portland to lose, to win the group outright. San Antonio may have a shot at the wild card spot if the Blazers and Spurs win.

#2866. Eagles-Cowboys final score: Joe Milton leads comeback victory over Dallas!

HAPPY THANKSGIVING, PHILADELPHIA EAGLES FANS!

The Eagles are 4-7 after beating the Dallas Cowboys on Thursday. Final score: 39 to 38.

Man, I can't believe the Eagles won that game! The Birds were looking left for dead after going down 31 to 10 with about three minutes remaining in the third quarter. Their defense was getting carved up by the Cowboys' high-powered offense.

But Joe Milton never said die! The Eagles' quarterback led his new team to a winning effort against his former team, ultimately finishing 21/31 for 331 yards, three touchdowns, one interception, and a 121.8 passer rating. A lot of that production came through DeVonta Smith, who had 11 catches for 200 yards (!) and two touchdowns.

Ty Robinson, who blocked a punt to create an Eagles touchdown earlier in the game, came through with a much-needed fourth down sack that ultimately set up a game-winning Jake Elliott field goal!

And suddenly the Eagles' season is not totally dead?! Could they actually win the NFC East?! Probably not … but they weren't supposed to win this game, either!

Next up for the Eagles: a road game against the Arizona Cardinals, which the Eagles will probably be favored in!

Read on for a recap and stay tuned for BGN's postgame coverage.

The Eagles won the coin toss and elected to defer, per usual. The Cowboys took over at their own 23-yard line and Quinyon Mitchell was flagged for pass interference on the first snap from scrimmage. Tacky call on Q going up against George Pickens, who was also pretty handsy on that play. Facing 3rd-and-3, a quick dump off to Javonte Williams resulted in a first down. Cooper DeJean was hurt after tackling Williams. The Cowboys picked up another first down into field goal range with Andrew Mukuba missing a tackle in the hole. Facing 3rd-and-8, Dak Prescott's first down scramble was called back by Jonathan Greenard drawing a holding penalty on Terence Steele. Facing 3rd-and-18, Prescott hit CeeDee Lamb wiiiiide open in the middle of the field for an easy first down at the seven-yard line. Great resistance there. Not! Jihaad Campbell seemed to get lost in coverage. The Cowboys then went with three straight runs to reach the end zone. 11 plays, 77 yards, 6:10 off the clock. EAGLES 0, COWBOYS 7.

The Eagles started at their own 6-yard line after Britain Covey mishandled Brandon Aubrey's bouncing kickoff. The Eagles picked up their first first down with a Joe Milton completion to Dallas Goedert. Facing 3rd-and-7 after a blitz forced a Milton incompletion, Milton ripped a nice ball to DeVonta Smith for another first down. A Cowboys player signaled incomplete, though, and Brian Schottenheimer threw his red flag to challenge the play. After commercial, it was announced that the Cowboys did not challenge in time before the Eagles got a play off. I don't know if the review would have been conclusive enough to overturn the ruling on the field anyway. Milton hit Elijah Moore (!) in rhythm for a 25-yard completion to put the offense past midfield. Milton balling early! Facing 3rd-and-6 after two Saquon Barkley runs, Milton hit DeVonta just shy of the sticks. Facing 4th-and-short, the Eagles seemed like they might go Brotherly Shove but they instead tried a fullback dive with Barkley and it was stopped for a loss. The decision to go for it was correct. Overthought it on the play call. Just use the tush push. Disappointing end to an otherwise encouraging 11-play, 57-yard drive.

The Cowboys took over at their own 37-yard line. Prescott's deep shot to Pickens with Riq Woolen trailing bounced off of the receiver for an incompletion. Fortunate break for the Eagles' defense. Facing 3rd-and-6, Prescott took off running for a first down but it was wiped out by another holding penalty on Javonte Williams grabbing Marcus Epps. Facing 3rd-and-16, Prescott hit Pickens for a contested grab against Woolen. Another favorable opportunity for the defense to get off the field and they just can't do it. One play later, Prescott hit KaVontae Turpin for a 31-yard catch-and-run touchdown. Too easy. Meanwhile, Nick Sirianni was shown arguing with Will Shipley on the sideline. OK, then. EAGLES 0, COWBOYS 14.

The Eagles took over at their own 15-yard line. Milton hit Zach Ertz (!) for a catch that got wiped out with the Eagles accepting a Cowboys illegal contact penalty to end the first quarter.

Facing 3rd-and-5, Milton ran out of bounds for no gain. Three-and-out. Honestly, should've gone for it there, even though it was on the Eagles' own 25-yard line.

The Cowboys over at their own 35-yard line after a 24-yard punt return by Turpin. Facing 3rd-and-5 after Jihaad Campbell had good coverage on Lamb (!), Prescott hit Turpin to beat an Eagles blitz for a first down. Turpin bobbled the ball, though, and Sirianni threw his red flag to challenge the play. After review, the call was overturned and the Cowboys had to punt. Hey, a stop! If only barely.

The Eagles took over at their own 18-yard line. DeVonta made a nice leaping catch against tight coverage from the Cowboys for 24 yards and a first down near midfield. Facing 3rd-and-12 after Markel Bell had a real rough rep to allow a Rashan Gary sack, Milton rolled right and hit a wide open Lemon for a first down! Milton got the juice?! Facing 3rd-and-6, Milton was blitzed and threw behind DeVonta for an incompletion. Jake Elliott came in for the 48-yard field goal make. I would've gone for it there, you're not beating the Cowboys by kicking FGs. But not a bad nine-play, 52-yard drive. EAGLES 3, COWBOYS 14.

The Cowboys took over at their own 40-yard line after their special teams made a smart play to draw an Eagles penalty. They picked up a first down with two runs, with Mukuba getting blocked out of the play on one. The Cowboys quickly moved into field goal range. And then into the red zone. Williams then went 12 yards easily up the gut for a touchdown. Zero resistance. EAGLES 3, COWBOYS 21.

The Eagles took over at their own 30-yard line. Ertz drew a pass interference penalty to give the Eagles a first down. Milton roll left and unleashed a rocket down the field to DeVonta for a 50-yard touchdown! Hell yeah! That was fun.

The Cowboys took over at their own 29-yard line after a nice special teams tackle by Darius Cooper. Facing 3rd-and-inches, Williams got enough on a first down run to then bleed the clock the two-minute warning. Q got flagged for illegal contact to allow another first down. Then Prescott hit Pickens for yet another. Woolen got flagged for a tacky call where him and Pickens were both handfighting. Lame. Then Q was flagged for pass interference with Lamb playing for the flag, not the ball. Lame flag. Javonte Williams then fumbled and Woolen recovered it with a path to the end zone but the refs ruled that his forward progress was stopped and the whistle was blown. Absolute bullshit. Williams was still fighting for yardage and the ball was out. Garbage. The Cowboys had a touchdown called back due to offensive pass interference on Lamb. Facing 3rd-and-goal at the 12-yard line, Prescott checked down to Williams and he found the end zone. Thanks for nothing, refs. EAGLES 10, COWBOYS 28.

The Eagles took a knee to end the first half.

The Eagles took over at their own 27-yard line. Facing 3rd-and-14 after Landon Dickerson got beat by Quinnen Williams to allow a sack, Milton spun out of pressure and fired a strike to Elijah Moore … who had the ball go through his hands. Bad and costly drop for what would've been a first down. Instead, three-and-out on three plays for negative four yards.

The Cowboys took over at their own 21-yard line. The Cowboys quickly moved near midfield with Mitchell allowing a 22-yard Lamb reception. Greenard shed a Tyler Guyton block for a TFL. Facing 3rd-and-7, a blitzing Jeremiah Trotter Jr. got to Prescott for a sack. Wow. Another defensive stop! Hard to believe.

The Eagles took over at their own 21-yard line. Two Shipley runs resulted in a first down. Facing 3rd-and-17 after Milton got sacked and then the Eagles decided to run a swing pass to Shipley in the flat for three yards, Milton's deep attempt to Moore was incomplete. No flag for pass interference despite the refs previously calling the game pretty tight, especially against the Eagles' favor.

The Cowboys took over at their own 41-yard line after a 33-yard punt return by Turpin. The Cowboys picked up a first down but got knocked back to 1st-and-20 after Lamb was called for offensive pass interference on Mitchell. Facing 3rd-and-10, Prescott hit Pickens for a first down on forward progress but the receiver ran backwards and ended up with a one-yard loss. Pretty crazy! Brandon Aubrey came in to drill the 59-yard field goal. EAGLES 10, COWBOYS 31.

The Eagles took over at their own 25-yard line. Milton hit DeVonta for a first down. Facing 3rd-and-5, milton scrambled and dumped off to Lemon, who got the first down before fortunately fumbling the ball out of bounds. Milton dropped back, rolled right, and launched a 45-yard bomb to DeVonta for another touchdown! Fun player, that Milton. EAGLES 17, COWBOYS 31.

The Cowboys took over at their own 26-yard line. Williams ran for five yards to end the third quarter. The ball came loose on the play and the Eagles had a shot to recover … but replay revealed that the runner was definitely downed before a fumble occurred anyway.

Facing 3rd-and-1, Jalen Carter got into the backfield to limit the Cowboys for no gain. Nice of JC to finally show up and log his first stat in the box score. The Cowboys tried to draw the Eagles offside before taking a delay of game penalty. Then Ty Robinson blocked the Cowboys' punt and Kenny Moore II scooped it for a touchdown! One-possession game! EAGLES 24, COWBOYS 31.

The Cowboys took over at their own 35-yard line. Carter got to Prescott for a sack! But then Prescott hit Lamb for a 39-yard gain with the receiver shaking Mitchell to force a missed tackle. Facing 3rd-and-8, Vic Fangio sent a blitz that only achieved leaving Lamb open over the middle for a first down into the red zone. Then Prescott took it to the end zone for a rushing touchdown. So much for an Eagles comeback. EAGLES 24, COWBOYS 38.

The Eagles took over at the Cowboys' 42-yard line after a 55-yard kick return by Covey. Milton hit DeVonta twice for a total of 27 yards and then Milton ran 15 yards untouched to the end zone! Lol, the Cowboys' defense is so bad. And, also, credit to Milton for making plays! The Eagles correctly went for the two-point conversion but Milton's pass to Ertz was incomplete. Would've liked a QB draw there, or some way to get Milton's legs involved. Regardless, a much-needed quick TD. That drive only took 1:02 off the clock. EAGLES 30, COWBOYS 38.

The Cowboys took over at their own 20-yard line after Jake Elliott's kickoff bounced at the one-yard line into the end zone for a shorter touchback (as opposed to the 35-yard line version). Nice kick. Greenard drew his second holding penalty of the day on the Cowboys' blocking. Facing 3rd-and-14, the Cowboys drew up a designed underneath catch and then lateral that resulted in an 84-yard touchdown … but the play was called back with the Cowboys flagged for offensive pass interference and an illegal forward pass on the attempted lateral. The latter penalty was accepted. Covey gained 23 yards on the punt return and it looked like he may have fumbled but he was ruled down. Schotty challenged the ruling and lost, as he should've. The ball was moving but Covey still had control of it as he was downed.

The Eagles took over at the Cowboys' 49-yard line. Milton made a great throw to DeVonta for a would-be over-the-shoulder catch but the receiver couldn't hold on while a Cowboys defender contested the ball as he was going the ground. Dang! Would've been an awesome grab. DeVonta was down in pain holding his back after the play. Facing 3rd-and-16 after Quinen Williams sacked Milton by beating the combination of Drew Kendall/Tyler Steen, Milton threw high of Lemon for an interception to effectively seal the game. Boo.

The Cowboys took over at the Eagles' 48-yard line. Facing 3rd-and-8 after moving to the 25-yard line, Prescott dumped off for three yards. Aubrey came in for the 40-yard field goal MISS. Hooked it left!

The Eagles took over at their own 30-yard line. Milton hit Darius Cooper (his first target of the game, finally) for a first down. The Eagles then quickly moved just outside of the red zone. Then Milton rolled right and found Lemon in the end zone!!! Wow. The Eagles went for two to tie … and Milton hit DeVonta … but Jordan Mailata's holding wiped out the score. The Eagles went for two again from the 12-yard line … but Milton's pass to Lemon was incomplete. Should've been DPI on Dallas! The Cowboys hit him early before the ball arrived. No flag. Dang. EAGLES 36, COWBOYS 38.

The Cowboys took over at their own 35-yard line. Rookie Cole "Brian Dawkins" Wisniewski flew down for a big hit in run defense. Wisniewski then had a really good contest on a catch made for a Cowboys first down anyway. Unfortunate. Facing 3rd-and-3 with the Eagles needing a stop to keep the game alive, they got it. Facing 4th-and-1, the Cowboys kept their offense on the field … and unsuccessfully tried to draw the Eagles offside. Facing 4th-and-1 again after calling a timeout, Prescott dropped back and got SACKED BY TY ROBINSON OH MY GOD. TURNOVER ON DOWNS.

The Eagles took over at their own 48-yard line with 0:50 on the clock and zero timeouts to work with. Milton's downfield pass was picked off but Jalen Thompson only got one foot in, thankfully. Milton completed a pass to DeVonta for 3rd-and-1 and then Milton's throw to Ertz drew a Cowboys for a first down at the Cowboys' 41-yard line. Then Markel Bell got called for the false start that wasn't a false start because that's what Lane Johnson always did. The refs then flew a flag for pass interference drawn by Shipley but the refs picked it up for being uncatchable. I don't know about that one. Jake Elliott came in for the 60-yard field goal attempt … AND HE FUCKING MADE IT!!! EAGLES 39, COWBOYS 38.

Elliott kicked the ball OUT OF THE END ZONE THIS TIME (unlike the Jags game) to give the Cowboys the ball at their own 35-yard line with 0:02 left. Mukuba picked off Prescott's desperation attempt to seal the game!

Michael Carter II was the backup nickel with Cooper DeJean getting banged up.

Cooper DeJean (concussion) got banged up after making a tackle on the Cowboys' first drive. He walked off the field on his own power. He was evaluated in the medical tent before walking into the tunnel for further evaluation. He was then quickly ruled out.

Dallas Goedert (shoulder) went into the tunnel and Tyler Steen was in the medical tent after the Eagles' first drive. Goedert was originally ruled questionable to return. Steen returned to the game without missing snaps. Goedert was later ruled out.

Saquon Barkley (stinger) got banged up late in the first quarter. He was ruled questionable to return. The broadcast said Barkley was lobbying to come back into the game but the training staff was keeping his helmet away from him. He ultimately did not return to the game. It's pretty silly that the Eagles didn't shut him down prior to this point.

Marcus Epps (foot) visited the medical tent in the early third quarter. He was carted into the locker room and ruled out.

Makai Lemon visited the medical tent late in the third quarter.

Gabe Hall (wrist) was banged up early in the fourth quarter. He was ruled questionable to return.

DeVonta Smith (back) got banged up midway through the fourth quarter after failing to make an over-the-shoulder catch and landing hard on the ground. Smitty quickly re-entered the game after a brief visit to the medical tent. DeVonta then left the field holding his back again.

#2865. Federal Reserve Board releases results from survey of senior financial officers at banks about their strategies and practices for managing reserve balances

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 10:00 a.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released results from a survey of senior financial officers at banks about their strategies and practices for managing reserve balances. The Senior Financial Officer Survey is used by the Board to obtain information about banks' reserve balance management strategies and practices, their expectations around the size of their balance sheets and deposit balances their activity in money markets, and their views on other matters, such as operations on weekends and holidays.

The survey was conducted in collaboration with the Federal Reserve Bank of New York between September 18, 2026, and September 28, 2026, and includes responses from banks that held around three-fourths of total banking system reserve balances at the time of the survey.

#2863. De’Anthony Melton stuffs the box score in Warriors’ pre-Thanksgiving win

On the first Thanksgiving, the overconfident Plymouth Rock settlers had to lean on the Wampanoag tribe to survive their first harvest in the New World. There's scant historical evidence about that first Thanksgiving, but some scholars believe that the Pilgrim colony found itself at a loss after Miles Standish suffered an abdominal oblique strain, one that wouldn't be evaluated until the Friday following Thanksgiving, a holiday that wouldn't exist for another quarter-millenium.

On the Wednesday before Thanksgiving 2026, the Golden State Warriors got 61 points from their bench players and eight three-pointers from De'Anthony Melton as they beat the Sacramento Kings, 129-110. Thanksgiving may not be a holiday in Brazil, but Gui Santos still feasted Wednesday night, putting up 22 points in 24 minutes.

Draymond Green had 11 assists, Yaxel Lendeborg had 15 points and eight rebounds and Brandin Podziemski notched a +16 in 32 minutes, but the Warriors won their game by dominating the bench minutes. Granted, bringing Leaky Black and Precious Achiuwa to an NBA game is like bringing a knife to a gun fight, especially if the knife turned the ball over 18 times, but the bench dominated, 61-19, to get the Warriors their second straight win.

That's intended in no way to minimize Melton, who is now shooting 44.9% from three-point range this season. He went 8-for-12 from distance Wednesday against the Kings and added seven assists, which just goes to show that any time you get the chance to have surgeries on both thumbs in one summer, you pull the damn trigger.

It was a game when the Warriors played well against type, taking a large 1st-quarter lead instead of falling hopelessly behind after eight minutes, a hallmark of their recent road trip. But they were back at the Chase Center, where the Warriors had won seven straight games after choking the home opener against the Memphis Grizzlies. And as the poet says, there's no place like home for the holidays.

Melton shot 4-for-5 from behind the arc in the opening period as the Warriors raced out to a 25-point lead after 12 minutes. They went 9-for-13 as a team and 14-for-22 overall, with Brandin Podziemski and Gui Santos each shooting 3-for-3 with two triples in the quarter.

Brandon Williams wasted no time in his first game in 16 days, scoring 14 points in 13 minutes, while putting up 14 shots and delivering three assists. That's officially a usage rate of "Thank God," and should help Williams when he lobbies the Chase Center's operations staff to change the current "road runner" sound effect when he scores to a Ferrari engine revving. He's now played 150 NBA games yet he already thinks he's too good for the road runner, a bird that has faced off against Wile E. Coyote and survived over a hundred times. Show him some repect!

In the second quarter, Melton made two more threes and Yaxel Lendeborg scored eight points to keep the halftime lead at 19 points. Melton also allowed Williams to use his laundry facilities this week, likely giving his teammate the confidence to dominate the ball the way he has. Williams does have his own apartment now, and more than perhaps any other Warrior needs them to advance to the prize money stage of the NBA Cup.

On the Sacramento side, Domantas Sabonis scored 27 points while taking only nine shots, also picking up 10 rebounds. Unfortunately for Sabonis, it's not clear if playing well or badly will get him what he truly wants: The chance to play for a team who isn't the 2-16 Kings.

Rookie Darius Acuff, Jr. had a high-volume, low-efficiency game, scoring 22 points on 9-for-24 shooting and logging nine assists. It's been a Make-A-Wish type season for Acuff, where the 20-year-old has been able to live his NBA dream of shooting whenever he wants, only it's also like he wished on a monkey's paw, because he's on a team that has won only two games all season.

But Gui Santos stood out with his first game of 20+ points all season, punishing smaller, slower, or just plain lazier Kings defenders to shoot 8-for-11 and score 22 points.

The Warriors are back up to 10-8, stuffing themselves on victories before heading to Portland Friday, where they'll face a team likely woozy from eating vegan turkey and giving out land acknowledgements all day Thursday. Could it be a three-game win streak for the Warriors? Maybe it's enough that the team is all together on a holiday. And still alive on the NBA Cup, which could even let Williams finally own his own washer.
